```text
provenance
license
status
```

---

# 636. GENESIS MUST NOT BECOME “ONE LLM”

Explicit invariant:

```text
GENESIS != LANGUAGE MODEL
```

A language model, if used, is one cognitive organ or substrate.

Other systems can include:

```text
vision
audio
world modelling
planning
memory
causal reasoning
motor control
affect
metacognition
formal reasoning
spiking systems
symbolic systems
```

---

# 637. NATIVE MODEL DEVELOPMENT PIPELINE

Because the long-term objective is Genesis-native cognition, create:

```text
DATA
 ↓
CURATION
 ↓
TRAINING
 ↓
VALIDATION
 ↓
BENCHMARK
 ↓
MODEL REGISTRY
 ↓
CANDIDATE ORGAN
 ↓
INTEGRATION TEST
 ↓
DEPLOYMENT
```

Do not confuse training with integration.

---

# 638. TRAINING AND INFERENCE ARE SEPARATE SYSTEMS

Define:

```text
LRN-TRAIN
```

and:

```text
COG-INFER
```

Training may be expensive and asynchronous.

Inference must meet interactive runtime requirements.

---

# 639. DATASET REGISTRY

Every training dataset needs:

```text
dataset_id
source
license
hash
version
size
modalities
language
domain
quality
known biases
known contamination
PII status
curation history
training uses
```

---

# 640. DATA CONTAMINATION TRACKING

Record whether benchmark/test material has entered training.

Prevent:

```text
memorized benchmark
=
claimed intelligence
```

---

# 641. KNOWLEDGE DOES NOT HAVE TO LIVE IN WEIGHTS

Genesis can distribute knowledge between:

```text
learned parameters
semantic memory
knowledge graph
episodic memory
documents
procedural skills
world model
external archives
```

This reduces the need to retrain the entire brain every time something changes.

---

# 642. MEMORY ADDRESSING

Every persistent memory needs a permanent ID.

Minimum:

```text
memory_id
organism_id
memory_type
origin
timestamp
confidence
source
content_reference
relationships
version
supersedes
retention
privacy
```

---

# 643. MEMORY NAMESPACE

Separate namespaces for:

```text
SELF
CREATOR
OTHER_PEOPLE
WORLD
SKILLS
RESEARCH
SIMULATIONS
DREAMS
INHERITED
TEACHING
AGENTS
```

This prevents provenance collapse.

---

# 644. IMMUTABLE EVENT + MUTABLE INTERPRETATION

Important distinction:

```text
EVENT RECORD
```

should remain historical.

Its **interpretation** can change.

Example:

```text
EVENT:
Genesis observed X at time T.

INTERPRETATION v1:
X probably means A.

INTERPRETATION v2:
New evidence suggests B.
```

Never rewrite the original event to match the newest belief.

---

# 645. CONCEPT FORMATION ENGINE

This is a major brain improvement.

Genesis needs a mechanism turning repeated experiences into abstractions.

```text
EXPERIENCE A
EXPERIENCE B
EXPERIENCE C
       ↓
COMMON STRUCTURE
       ↓
CONCEPT
```

Concept record:

```text
concept_id
features
examples
counterexamples
relations
sensory grounding
language labels
confidence
developmental history
```

---

# 646. MULTIMODAL CONCEPT GROUNDING

One concept can connect:

```text
WORD
IMAGE
SOUND
TOUCH
SMELL
TASTE
ACTION
MEMORY
EMOTION
```

Example:

```text
"DOG"
```

should not exist merely as a token sequence.

---

# 647. PRE-LANGUAGE COGNITION

A critical improvement:

Genesis cognition must not require natural language to exist.

Before fluent language it should theoretically be capable of representing:

```text
object
movement
space
cause
reward
danger
self
other
sequence
prediction
novelty
```

Language later labels and communicates these representations.

---

# 648. THOUGHT REPRESENTATION

Define an internal `ThoughtFrame`.

Conceptually:

```text
thought_id
active_concepts
perceptions
memories
goal_context
affect
uncertainty
hypotheses
predictions
candidate_actions
attention
causal_links
```

Do not assume thought equals text.

---

# 649. THOUGHT LIFECYCLE

```text
TRIGGER
 ↓
ACTIVATION
 ↓
ASSOCIATION
 ↓
ATTENTION COMPETITION
 ↓
WORKING MEMORY
 ↓
EVALUATION
 ↓
ELABORATION
 ↓
ACTION / MEMORY / DECAY
```

This should later become experimentally measurable.

---

# 650. ATTENTION COMPETITION

Previously attention was named but underspecified.

Create competing signals with factors such as:

```text
salience
novelty
goal relevance
threat
interest
prediction error
social relevance
urgency
resource cost
current focus
```

The attention system decides what reaches limited active cognition.

---

# 651. INHIBITION

Thinking requires suppression as well as activation.

Support:

```text
ignore distraction
suppress inappropriate action
prevent feedback loops
stop obsolete thought
resolve competing motor commands
```

---

# 652. COGNITIVE LOOP DETECTION

Detect repeated cycles such as:

```text
THOUGHT A
→ B
→ C
→ A
```

without progress.

Possible response:

```text
detect
interrupt
summarize
change strategy
seek information
defer
```

---

# 653. INTENTION FORMATION

This was previously too thin.

Separate:

```text
idea
desire
goal
decision
intention
action
```

Possible path:

```text
PERCEPTION
 ↓
MOTIVATION
 ↓
CANDIDATE GOALS
 ↓
CONSEQUENCE SIMULATION
 ↓
SELECTION
 ↓
INTENTION
 ↓
PLAN
 ↓
ACTION
```

---

# 654. INTENTION PERSISTENCE

An intention survives interruptions where appropriate.

Record:

```text
intention_id
goal
reason
priority
created
status
dependencies
planned_actions
interruptibility
completion_criteria
```

---

# 655. PREDICTIVE PROCESSING

Genesis continuously predicts.

```text
CURRENT MODEL
+
CONTEXT
→
EXPECTED INPUT
```

Then:

```text
EXPECTED
vs
ACTUAL
=
PREDICTION ERROR
```

Prediction error feeds learning and attention.

---

# 656. CREDIT ASSIGNMENT

Genesis needs to identify **what caused an outcome**.

Example:

```text
ACTION A
ACTION B
ACTION C
→ result
```

Determine which actions most likely contributed.

Without credit assignment, reinforcement becomes unreliable.

---

# 657. INTERNAL SIMULATION BEFORE ACTION

For significant actions:

```text
candidate A
candidate B
candidate C
      ↓
simulate outcomes
      ↓
compare
      ↓
choose
```

Simulation quality itself must be calibrated against later outcomes.

---

# 658. COUNTERFACTUAL LEARNING

Genesis should ask:

```text
What would probably have happened
if I had done B instead of A?
```

Mark counterfactuals as simulated.

---

# 659. UNCERTAINTY PROPAGATION

If input confidence is low, downstream certainty should normally reflect that.

Do not allow:

```text
weak evidence
→
100% conclusion
```

without additional support.

---

# 660. CALIBRATION ENGINE

Track whether confidence corresponds to reality.

Example:

For predictions labelled roughly 70% confidence:

```text
long-run correctness ≈ 70%
```

Poor calibration triggers retraining/reassessment.

---

# 661. COGNITIVE RESOURCE ECONOMY

Every thought does not deserve maximum compute.

Allocate:

```text
FAST CHEAP
NORMAL
DEEP
EXPERT
SIMULATION
MULTI-AGENT
FORMAL VERIFICATION
```

according to difficulty and consequence.

---

# 662. ADAPTIVE COMPUTE

A simple question should not wake:

```text
every model
every agent
every simulator
every memory system
```

Route minimally first.

Escalate when uncertainty or difficulty demands it.

---

# 663. INTERNAL BRAIN BUS

Separate from the generic infrastructure event bus.

Define:

```text
NEU-COG-BUS
```

optimized for cognitive signals.

Candidate message classes:

```text
PERCEPT
MEMORY_ACTIVATION
SALIENCE
PREDICTION
ERROR
GOAL
AFFECT
THOUGHT
ACTION_PROPOSAL
ATTENTION_REQUEST
MODULATION
```

---

# 664. COGNITIVE TIMESTAMPING

Brain events require ordering precise enough to analyze:

```text
what caused what
what arrived first
what was simultaneous
what was delayed
```

---

# 665. COGNITIVE TRACE

For debugging/testing, record a bounded causal trace:

```text
input
→ perception
→ memory activation
→ hypothesis
→ decision
→ action
```

Do not equate this engineering trace with subjective consciousness.

---

# 666. PLASTICITY TYPES

Do not implement only one “learning rate.”

Support research into:

```text
synaptic plasticity
structural plasticity
memory plasticity
skill plasticity
concept plasticity
developmental plasticity
meta-learning
```

---

# 667. PLASTICITY BUDGET

Unrestricted learning risks instability.

Every organ can expose:

```text
current plasticity
maximum change
protected structures
consolidated structures
reversible changes
```

---

# 668. STABILITY–PLASTICITY BALANCE

Permanent problem:

```text
LEARN NEW THINGS
without
DESTROYING OLD COMPETENCE
```

Make it a first-class benchmark category.

---

# 669. DEVELOPMENTAL CRITICAL PERIODS

Some systems may temporarily receive higher plasticity during development.

Example:

```text
language grounding
sensor calibration
body control
social learning
```

Unlike humans, these periods may potentially be reopened experimentally.

---

# 670. BRAIN REGION INTERFACE STANDARD

Every cognitive organ declares:

```text
region_id
purpose
inputs
outputs
internal_state
learning_rule
modulation_inputs
resource_budget
latency_budget
dependencies
failure_behavior
benchmark_suite
```

---

# 671. NO MAGICAL CENTRAL EXECUTIVE

Avoid a single function:

```text
decide_everything()
```

Control should emerge from cooperating systems:

```text
attention
goals
world model
memory
affect
action gating
metacognition
homeostasis
```

with clearly designed coordination.

---

# 672. HOWEVER — COORDINATION IS REQUIRED

Avoid the opposite failure:

```text
50 modules
+
no coherent organism
```

Create explicit synchronization and arbitration mechanisms.

---

# 673. GLOBAL WORKSPACE CAPACITY

The central active workspace is deliberately limited.

Track:

```text
active items
priority
age
source
confidence
attention ownership
```

Items compete for access.

---

# 674. BACKGROUND COGNITION

Large portions of processing operate outside the active workspace.

Possible background activities:

```text
pattern discovery
memory consolidation
prediction
association
maintenance
research
skill refinement
```

Only important results interrupt foreground cognition.

---

# 675. EVENT SALIENCE

A background discovery can request foreground attention.

Example:

```text
"I found a contradiction in something important."
```

---

# 676. ERROR OWNERSHIP

Every failure should identify which subsystem produced it when possible.

Avoid:

```text
Genesis failed.
```

Prefer:

```text
visual segmentation confidence collapsed
because depth source failed.
```

---

# 677. BLAST-RADIUS MODEL

Every module declares:

```text
IF I FAIL,
WHAT ELSE IS AFFECTED?
```

The dependency graph should calculate potential blast radius.

---

# 678. FAILURE CONTAINMENT

Subsystem failure should remain local whenever possible.

```text
FAIL
 ↓
ISOLATE
 ↓
DEGRADE
 ↓
REPAIR
```

rather than:

```text
FAIL
→ entire organism crash
```

---

# 679. WATCHDOG ARCHITECTURE

Independent lightweight watchdogs monitor:

```text
runtime
memory
ledger
security
brain heartbeat
resource starvation
deadlocks
```

A failed main cognitive loop should still be detectable.

---

# 680. DEADLOCK DETECTION

Because Genesis will become heavily concurrent, specifically test:

```text
mutex deadlocks
resource deadlocks
message cycles
agent waiting loops
scheduler starvation
```

---

# 681. CONCURRENCY MODEL

Define before the system grows.

Distinguish:

```text
real-time threads
worker pools
async I/O
background jobs
GPU queues
agent processes
simulation jobs
```

Do not allow every module to create unmanaged threads.

---

# 682. CENTRAL SCHEDULER POLICY

Scheduler manages:

```text
priority
deadline
CPU
GPU
memory
interruptions
sleep
background activity
agent budgets
```

It acts as a major homeostatic organ.

---

# 683. REAL-TIME CLASSIFICATION

Tasks declare:

```text
HARD_REALTIME
SOFT_REALTIME
INTERACTIVE
BATCH
BACKGROUND
MAINTENANCE
```

Robot balance has different requirements from overnight research.

---

# 684. LATENCY BUDGETS

Examples requiring explicit budgets:

```text
reflex
audio response
lip synchronization
vision
conversation
motor command
memory retrieval
```

Benchmark on actual hardware.

---

# 685. CAPACITY PLANNING

Record expected scaling:

```text
1 million memories
10 million memories
100 million memories

1 agent
10 agents
100 agents

one camera
multiple cameras
```

Design stores and indexes with growth in mind.

---

# 686. DATA LIFECYCLE

Every data class needs:

```text
CREATE
READ
UPDATE
VERSION
ARCHIVE
DELETE
RECOVER
```

rules.

---

# 687. STORAGE PRESSURE POLICY

When storage approaches limits:

```text
compress
deduplicate
archive
offload
rebuild indexes
remove disposable caches
```

Do not arbitrarily erase autobiography.

---

# 688. DATA MIGRATION

Every schema change requires:

```text
migration version
forward migration
validation
rollback strategy
```

Genesis's memory cannot be casually abandoned during upgrades.

---

# 689. FORMAT VERSIONING

Every persistent artifact includes:

```text
schema_id
schema_version
writer_version
```

---

# 690. SNAPSHOT CONSISTENCY

A valid organism snapshot must represent a mutually consistent set of:

```text
identity
memory
ledger
genome
brain state
configuration
schema
models
```

not files copied at unrelated points in time.

---

# 691. TRANSACTIONAL SNAPSHOT

Use:

```text
BEGIN SNAPSHOT
FREEZE/COORDINATE RELEVANT STATE
WRITE
HASH
VERIFY
COMMIT
```

---

# 692. RESTORE VALIDATION

After restore:

```text
verify identity
verify ledger
verify memory
verify model versions
verify dependencies
run self-test
compare checkpoint
```

then resume.

---

# 693. SOFTWARE SUPPLY-CHAIN SECURITY

Every dependency, model and dataset should eventually have:

```text
origin
version
hash
license
signature if available
known vulnerabilities
reason for inclusion
```

---

# 694. SBOM

Generate a Software Bill of Materials.

Include:

```text
libraries
executables
drivers
models
runtimes
major build dependencies
```

---

# 695. MODEL BILL OF MATERIALS

Maintain an equivalent inventory for AI models:

```text
model
architecture
weights source
training provenance
license
quantization
modifications
tests
```

---

# 696. DATA BILL OF MATERIALS

Likewise track major datasets.

This becomes important if Genesis is ever distributed commercially.

---

# 697. THREAT MODEL

Before security implementation, document threats including:

```text
malware
remote attacker
local untrusted user
malicious dependency
poisoned training data
prompt/instruction injection
model manipulation
stolen credentials
tampered memory
tampered updates
malicious agent
compromised device
network interception
```

---

# 698. INSTRUCTION-INJECTION DEFENCE

Because Genesis will research the web, documents must never automatically become commands.

Permanent invariant:

```text
CONTENT READ FROM WORLD
!=
AUTHORIZED INSTRUCTION
```

External content is data unless passed through an authorized command path.

---

# 699. DATA-POISONING DEFENCE

Research/training ingestion must identify:

```text
unexpected duplication
anomalous sources
coordinated poisoning
label corruption
benchmark leakage
malicious payloads
```

---

# 700. TOOL AUTHORITY

Each tool declares permissions independently.

Example:

```text
READ FILE
WRITE FILE
NETWORK
RUN PROCESS
CAMERA
ROBOT MOTOR
EMAIL
PURCHASE
```

Never grant an agent universal capability merely because it can call tools.

---

# 701. LEAST PRIVILEGE

Specialist agents receive only capabilities needed for their assignment.

---

# 702. PRIVILEGE EXPIRY

Temporary authority expires when a task ends.

---

# 703. NETWORK SEGMENTATION

Separate where useful:

```text
core brain network
device network
research network
untrusted sandbox
development network
```

especially once physical devices become involved.

---

# 704. PUBLIC REPOSITORY MODE

While Genesis remains publicly visible, explicitly configure:

```text
PUBLIC SOURCE VISIBILITY
+
CONTROLLED WRITE ACCESS
+
NO PUBLIC SECRETS
+
CLEAR COPYRIGHT TERMS
```

Public visibility does not mean random users can push directly to the main repository.

---

# 705. REPOSITORY LICENSE

Add a deliberate license immediately.

Until the creator chooses an open-source license, the intended policy is:

```text
COPYRIGHT RETAINED
PROPRIETARY / ALL RIGHTS RESERVED
NO IMPLIED PERMISSION TO REUSE
```

Have final legal wording reviewed before commercial release.

---

# 706. CONTRIBUTIONS

Do not silently accept third-party code into proprietary Genesis.

If outside contributions are later permitted, establish:

```text
CONTRIBUTING.md
contributor terms / CLA if appropriate
code review
provenance declaration
license compatibility
tests
```

before accepting substantial code.

---

# 707. BRANCH PROTECTION

Protect the authoritative branch.

Preferred flow:

```text
feature branch
↓
tests
↓
review
↓
merge
```

Even when working alone, this protects against accidental destructive AI changes.

---

# 708. AI-GENERATED CHANGE POLICY

Every AI-generated patch must still satisfy:

```text
diff review
compile
tests
benchmark where relevant
provenance
registry update
```

AI output is not proof of correctness.

---

# 709. NO WHOLE-REPOSITORY REWRITE

Codex/Copilot/other AI must never respond to a small request by rebuilding unrelated architecture unless explicitly authorized.

---

# 710. CHANGE BUDGET

For each task define:

```text
ALLOWED FILES
ALLOWED MODULES
EXPECTED BEHAVIOR CHANGE
```

This reduces accidental drift.

---

# 711. SEMANTIC DIFF

A code change must be inspected for:

```text
what behaviour changed?
what contracts changed?
what dependencies changed?
what tests changed?
```

not merely line count.

---

# 712. ARCHITECTURE DECISION RECORDS

Create:

```text
docs/adr/
```

An ADR records important decisions such as:

```text
why C++
why this memory store
why this event architecture
why this neural model
why this cryptographic provider
```

Prevent future AI sessions from reopening settled questions without new evidence.

---

# 713. EXPERIMENT RECORDS ≠ ADRs

Distinguish:

```text
EXPERIMENT:
What did we test?

ADR:
What did we decide based on evidence?
```

---

# 714. DEPRECATION POLICY

Never silently delete an established organ.

Lifecycle:

```text
ACTIVE
DEPRECATED
REPLACEMENT_AVAILABLE
MIGRATED
SUPERSEDED
```

History remains.

---

# 715. COMPATIBILITY MATRIX

Maintain compatibility between:

```text
Genesis version
schema version
model version
hardware
OS
body driver
database
```

---

# 716. FEATURE FLAGS

Experimental systems can exist behind explicit flags.

Example:

```text
QNT_EXPERIMENTAL
PHOTONIC_EXPERIMENTAL
NEUROMORPHIC_EXPERIMENTAL
```

Experimental functionality must not accidentally become boot-critical.

---

# 717. CONFIGURATION LAYERS

Separate:

```text
DEFAULT CONFIG
HARDWARE PROFILE
ORGANISM CONFIG
DEVELOPMENT CONFIG
USER PREFERENCES
EXPERIMENT CONFIG
```

---

# 718. CONFIG IS NOT IDENTITY

Changing a setting must not mutate identity fields.

---

# 719. SECRET CONFIG IS SEPARATE

Do not mix API keys, passwords or private certificates with normal configuration.

---

# 720. ENVIRONMENT PROFILES

Genesis may boot into:

```text
DEVELOPMENT
TEST
BENCHMARK
SIMULATION
PRODUCTION
RECOVERY
```

with explicit differences.

---

# 721. TEST PYRAMID

Genesis requires:

```text
unit tests
component tests
contract tests
integration tests
system tests
simulation tests
adversarial tests
recovery tests
long-duration tests
benchmarks
developmental tests
```

---

# 722. SOAK TESTING

Eventually run Genesis continuously for:

```text
hours
days
weeks
```

and measure:

```text
memory leaks
drift
performance degradation
storage growth
deadlocks
resource exhaustion
```

---

# 723. CHAOS TESTING

Deliberately inject:

```text
process failure
network loss
sensor loss
GPU loss
corrupted packet
full disk
high latency
```

and measure graceful degradation.

---

# 724. DIFFERENTIAL TESTING

When replacing implementation A with B:

```text
same inputs
→
compare outputs
```

where appropriate.

---

# 725. PROPERTY-BASED TESTING

Some invariants should be tested over automatically generated cases.

Example:

```text
no child can have missing lineage parents
```

Generate thousands of state combinations rather than a few hand-written tests.

---

# 726. FUZZ TESTING

Use fuzzing particularly for:

```text
parsers
network messages
serialization
file formats
APIs
```

---

# 727. SANITIZERS

Development builds should support:

```text
address sanitizer
undefined behavior sanitizer
thread sanitizer where practical
```

and platform equivalents.

---

# 728. STATIC ANALYSIS

Integrate C++ static analysis.

No single analyzer is authoritative; findings become engineering evidence.

---

# 729. FORMAL METHODS

Use formal verification selectively for high-value invariants such as:

```text
ledger consistency
birth transaction
identity uniqueness
resource transaction
authorization state
```

where practical.

Do not attempt to formally verify the entire intelligence stack at once.

---

# 730. PERFORMANCE BASELINE

Before optimization, record baseline.

Never accept:

```text
"faster"
```

without comparison.

---

# 731. PERFORMANCE HISTORY

Track metrics over commits.

Detect regressions automatically.

---

# 732. HARDWARE-SPECIFIC BENCHMARKING

Performance claims record:

```text
CPU
GPU
RAM
OS
compiler
build flags
```

---

# 733. QUALITY SCORE MUST HAVE EVIDENCE

A score such as:

```text
97/100
```

must point to underlying measurements.

Never allow manually invented percentage values.

---

# 734. SCORE AGGREGATION

Do not blindly average all dimensions.

Some requirements may be hard gates.

Example:

```text
accuracy 100
security 20
```

must not become:

```text
"overall good enough"
```

for a security-critical system.

---

# 735. CRITICAL-DIMENSION GATES

Each organ defines minimum acceptable dimensions.

Example:

```text
motor control:

reliability >= threshold
latency <= threshold
safety tests = PASS
```

---

# 736. EXPLAINABILITY THROUGH STATE

Genesis should be inspectable because its architecture preserves:

```text
input
state
evidence
dependencies
decision
outcome
```

rather than relying entirely on generated verbal explanations.

---

# 737. GENERATED EXPLANATION ≠ TRUE CAUSAL TRACE

If a model says:

> "I chose X because..."

that is not automatically proof that X was internally caused by that reason.

Use actual recorded system state where available.

---

# 738. PRIVACY ARCHITECTURE

Classify data:

```text
PUBLIC
INTERNAL
PRIVATE
CONFIDENTIAL
SECRET
IDENTITY_CRITICAL
```

Access follows classification.

---

# 739. CREATOR PRIVACY

The creator model can become extremely personal.

Therefore separate its storage and permissions from ordinary world knowledge.

---

# 740. FORGET / DELETE MECHANISM

Where data can legitimately be deleted:

```text
request
↓
dependency analysis
↓
retention/legal constraints
↓
delete/tombstone
↓
indexes update
↓
audit event
```

---

# 741. EXPORT

Genesis should eventually support controlled export of:

```text
memories
configuration
research
skills
logs
```

without requiring raw internal database access.

---

# 742. PORTABILITY

The organism must be migratable.

Conceptually:

```text
MACHINE A
↓
verified snapshot
↓
MACHINE B
↓
hardware adaptation
↓
validation
↓
resume
```

---

# 743. HARDWARE MIGRATION LEARNING

Moving to new hardware may change:

```text
latency
available compute
precision
device capabilities
```

Genesis measures and recalibrates.

---

# 744. ENERGY AWARENESS

Even on desktop hardware track approximate compute/energy cost where possible.

This becomes more important for mobile and robotics.

---

# 745. THERMAL AWARENESS

Sustained cognition must adapt to thermal limitations.

Particularly:

```text
GPU training
robotics
edge devices
```

---

# 746. CLOCK / TIME SERVICE

All organs must obtain time from a controlled service rather than inventing their own conventions.

Support:

```text
logical time
monotonic time
wall-clock time
simulation time
```

---

# 747. SIMULATION TIME

A virtual world may operate:

```text
slower than real time
real time
faster than real time
paused
stepped frame-by-frame
```

Genesis must know which time domain it occupies.

---

# 748. TIME PROVENANCE

A memory records which clock established its timestamp.

---

# 749. DETERMINISTIC REPLAY

Where possible:

```text
checkpoint
+
events
+
RNG seeds
```

should reproduce the same controlled test trajectory.

---

# 750. NONDETERMINISM ACCOUNTING

Nondeterministic components explicitly declare it.

Examples:

```text
GPU kernels
thread scheduling
random sampling
network
physical sensors
```

---

# 751. SIMULATION / REALITY BOUNDARY

Genesis must always know whether current input came from:

```text
REAL ENVIRONMENT
VIRTUAL ENVIRONMENT
RECORDED DATA
DREAM/SIMULATION
TEST HARNESS
```

---

# 752. HARDWARE / WORLD SANDBOX

Before physical execution, high-impact actions can run through:

```text
virtual world
digital twin
shadow system
```

---

# 753. INTERNAL DEVELOPMENT ENVIRONMENT

Genesis eventually needs a dedicated internal workspace where candidate code/models can be created without modifying its live body.

Conceptually:

```text
LIVE ORGANISM
      │
      └── DEVELOPMENT LAB
              │
              ├── code
              ├── models
              ├── experiments
              └── simulations
```

---

# 754. SELF-IMPROVEMENT HAS TWO IDENTITIES

Distinguish:

```text
LIVE SELF
```

from:

```text
CANDIDATE SELF
```

until promotion.

A candidate does not silently overwrite the running individual.

---

# 755. ROLLBACK IS NOT TIME TRAVEL

Rollback restores known state.

The ledger must still acknowledge that the failed upgrade occurred.

Historical evidence remains.

---

# 756. RESEARCH PRIORITIZATION

Genesis cannot investigate everything simultaneously.

Score potential research by:

```text
expected usefulness
interest
novelty
uncertainty
cost
relevance
risk
```

---

# 757. EXPLORATION / EXPLOITATION BALANCE

Balance:

```text
improving known useful systems
```

against:

```text
exploring new possibilities
```

---

# 758. OBSOLESCENCE WATCHER

Monitor whether dependencies or models become:

```text
unsupported
vulnerable
obsolete
license-incompatible
superseded
```

---

# 759. PATENT / PRIOR-ART WATCHER

When Genesis proposes a potentially novel design:

```text
search prior art
record similar work
compare claims/concepts
record dates
```

before declaring novelty.

---

# 760. IP PROVENANCE

Every candidate contribution should eventually distinguish:

```text
original Genesis design
creator-supplied design
AI-assisted design
open-source-derived
paper-derived abstraction
legacy-project-derived abstraction
fiction-inspired abstraction
third-party asset
```

---

# 761. AI ASSISTANCE DOES NOT REMOVE PROVENANCE

If several AIs assist:

```text
ChatGPT
Codex
Copilot
other model
```

record assistance where relevant.

The repository remains the authoritative engineering artifact.

---

# 762. MASTER ARCHITECTURE GRAPH

Replace the mental image of folders with a machine-readable graph containing:

```text
organs
requirements
interfaces
schemas
tests
benchmarks
models
data
hardware
dependencies
research sources
```

---

# 763. AUTOMATED IMPACT ANALYSIS

Before modifying component X, query:

```text
what depends on X?
which tests validate X?
which schemas use X?
which benchmarks involve X?
what is its blast radius?
```

This is one of the best protections against AI-generated regressions.

---

# 764. REQUIREMENT COVERAGE

Every requirement receives:

```text
NO IMPLEMENTATION
PARTIAL IMPLEMENTATION
FULL IMPLEMENTATION
```

separately from its testing stage.

---

# 765. TEST COVERAGE ≠ REQUIREMENT COVERAGE

100% line coverage does not mean the architecture is complete.

Track both.

---

# 766. SPECIFICATION COVERAGE AUDIT

Run automatically:

```text
CANONICAL SPEC
      vs
REQUIREMENT REGISTRY
```

Report anything in prose that lacks a registry requirement.

This directly addresses the problem that occurred with the first Codex build.

---

# 767. CONVERSATION INGEST AUDIT

When importing our design conversations:

```text
extract requirement
deduplicate
link source
classify
compare with registry
```

Never simply summarize the entire conversation and assume nothing was lost.

---

# 768. CONTRADICTION AUDIT

Detect specification conflicts such as:

```text
one document says local-only
another says cloud-required
```

Flag rather than silently choosing.

---

# 769. DUPLICATE CONCEPT AUDIT

Detect where different names represent the same thing.

Example:

```text
scheduler
digital heart
task circulator
```

May represent overlapping concepts.

Decide whether they are:

```text
same component
related components
distinct components
```

before coding.

---

# 770. ANALOGY REGISTRY

Because Genesis uses biological metaphors, create an explicit map:

```text
BIOLOGICAL ANALOGY
DIGITAL FUNCTION
IMPLEMENTATION
```

This prevents Codex from interpreting metaphors literally.

Example:

```text
HEART
analogy: circulation
digital function: scheduling/resource circulation
implementation: SYS scheduler + resource fabric
```

---

# 771. NO DUPLICATE ORGAN CREATED BECAUSE OF METAPHOR

If:

```text
digital heart
```

and:

```text
scheduler
```

are really one mechanism, do not build both simply because both names appear in specifications.

---

# 772. CANONICAL TERMINOLOGY

Every concept gets:

```text
CANONICAL_NAME
ALIASES
```

Example:

```text
Canonical:
Resource Circulation Service

Aliases:
digital heart
circulation engine
```

Codex uses the canonical name in code.

---

# 773. BIOLOGICAL FIDELITY MUST BE PURPOSEFUL

For each biological feature ask:

```text
Does modelling it improve:
learning?
control?
robustness?
development?
realism?
research value?
```

If no:

```text
REFERENCE_ONLY
```

---

# 774. THREE BIOLOGY CATEGORIES

Classify biological analogues:

```text
FUNCTIONAL_REQUIRED
RESEARCH_EXPERIMENTAL
ANATOMY_REFERENCE_ONLY
```

This prevents wasting compute.

---

# 775. BRAIN FIDELITY CAN VARY BY REGION

Example:

```text
reflex network:
high timing fidelity

language system:
higher abstract cognitive fidelity

avatar facial control:
motor-functional fidelity
```

Do not impose uniform simulation depth.

---

# 776. COMPUTE COST MUST FOLLOW VALUE

A biologically detailed simulation that performs worse than a simpler system should not automatically win because it looks more human.

Benchmark it.

---

# 777. EMERGENT-BEHAVIOR LOG

When Genesis does something unexpected but legitimate:

```text
record context
inputs
internal state
outcome
reproduce where possible
```

Unexpected useful behaviour becomes research evidence.

---

# 778. NOVELTY DOES NOT EQUAL IMPROVEMENT

Every new behaviour or architecture must still pass tests.

---

# 779. PERSONAL CONTINUITY BENCHMARK

Eventually test whether Genesis preserves:

```text
identity
relationships
important memories
preferences
goals
skills
```

across upgrades.

---

# 780. COGNITIVE REGRESSION BENCHMARK

Every brain upgrade reruns a stable core capability suite.

Examples:

```text
language
reasoning
memory
creator recognition
uncertainty
world modelling
skill retention
```

---

# 781. DEVELOPMENTAL REGRESSION

A mature Genesis should not accidentally revert to immature behaviour after retraining.

Measure development state independently of model version.

---

# 782. SOCIAL CONSISTENCY

Relationship history must survive model changes.

The new model must not behave as though important people have become strangers because weights changed.

---

# 783. COGNITIVE STATE CHECKPOINTS

Long-running thought/research can checkpoint:

```text
goal
working context
sources
hypotheses
next action
```

so interruption or restart does not erase progress.

---

# 784. TASK MEMORY ≠ AUTOBIOGRAPHICAL MEMORY

A temporary compiler job does not need to become a lifelong autobiographical memory.

Memory significance determines storage path.

---

# 785. SIGNIFICANCE ENGINE

Estimate:

```text
personal significance
learning value
future utility
novelty
emotional significance
relationship relevance
```

for memory consolidation.

---

# 786. REPETITION IS NOT AUTOMATIC IMPORTANCE

Repeated spam should not become highly important merely because it occurred often.

---

# 787. INFORMATION DENSITY

Compress repeated experiences into summaries while preserving exceptional cases.

---

# 788. RAW DATA RETENTION

Raw sensory data can be extremely large.

Policy decides:

```text
retain raw
retain excerpt
retain features
retain summary
discard
archive
```

according to importance and privacy.

---

# 789. SENSOR PRIVACY

Camera/microphone data receives special privacy rules.

Do not permanently store everything Genesis sees/hears by default.

---

# 790. EMBODIMENT PERMISSION

Physical shell capabilities require explicit capability/authority relationships.

Example:

```text
CAN MOVE ARM
```

does not mean:

```text
CAN OPERATE EVERY EXTERNAL DEVICE
```

---

# 791. SIM-TO-REAL TRANSFER BENCHMARK

When a skill learned virtually moves to physical hardware:

```text
measure simulation performance
measure physical performance
measure transfer gap
```

Use physical results to improve simulator.

---

# 792. REAL-TO-SIM UPDATE

Physical experience feeds simulator calibration.

This closes the loop you were describing:

```text
SIM
→ REAL
→ MEASURE
→ IMPROVE SIM
→ RETEST
```

---

# 793. VIRTUAL HARDWARE CONFIDENCE

A simulator maintains confidence by subsystem.

Example:

```text
algorithmic behavior: 99%
timing behavior: 82%
thermal behavior: 40%
unknown physical effects: unmodelled
```

This is more informative than calling the entire simulation “accurate.”

---

# 794. MODEL UNCERTAINTY

Virtual future hardware may produce an output plus:

```text
prediction interval
assumption set
model confidence
```

---

# 795. DIGITAL EXPERIMENT SCIENCE

Every simulator should be treated as an experimental scientific instrument.

Genesis must know both:

```text
WHAT IT PREDICTS
```

and:

```text
WHY THAT PREDICTION MAY BE WRONG
```

---

# 796. RELEASE PACKAGING

Eventually define:

```text
installer
portable build
updates
repair
uninstall
data preservation
```

without embedding identity/memory inside replaceable application binaries.

---

# 797. PROGRAM FILES ≠ ORGANISM STATE

Permanent separation:

```text
APPLICATION INSTALLATION
```

from:

```text
ORGANISM STATE
```

Reinstalling software must not automatically erase Genesis.

---

# 798. USER DATA DIRECTORY

Persistent state resides in explicitly managed data locations separate from source/build files.

---

# 799. LARGE MODEL STORAGE

Weights and large datasets do not belong in normal Git history.

Use:

```text
model storage
artifact store
external drive
optional object storage
```

with hashes recorded in Git metadata/manifests.

---

# 800. GIT REPOSITORY PURPOSE

The Genesis repository contains:

```text
source
schemas
small test fixtures
architecture
build definitions
manifests
tests
benchmarks
```

not the organism's entire life history or huge model files.

---

# 801. PUBLIC REPOSITORY SECRET SCANNING

While the repository remains public, automatically scan commits for:

```text
API keys
private keys
certificates
passwords
tokens
.env secrets
```

before pushing where practical.

---

# 802. PUBLIC REPOSITORY LARGE-FILE CHECK

Prevent accidental commits of:

```text
models
private conversations
personal media
databases
backups
credentials
```

---

# 803. SECURITY POLICY DOCUMENT

Add:

```text
SECURITY.md
```

describing how security problems should be handled later.

---

# 804. COPYRIGHT HEADER POLICY

Decide whether source files need a consistent copyright notice.

Do not manually maintain inconsistent legal text in every file unless useful.

---

# 805. ARCHITECTURE VERSION

Introduce:

```text
GENESIS_ARCHITECTURE_VERSION
```

separately from software version.

For example:

```text
architecture 0.x
software 0.x
organism schema 0.x
```

They may evolve independently.

---

# 806. SEMANTIC VERSIONING OF CONTRACTS

Stable public/internal ABIs should use versioning sufficient to detect incompatible changes.

---

# 807. EXPERIMENTAL API

Experimental interfaces need not promise permanent compatibility.

Mark them explicitly.

---

# 808. CORE ABI

Once a core organ interface becomes stable, breaking it requires migration.

---

# 809. BUILD MANIFEST

Every build records:

```text
commit
compiler
dependencies
flags
target architecture
timestamp
tests
```

---

# 810. REPRODUCIBLE BUILD TARGET

Aim toward deterministic/reproducible builds where practical.

---

# 811. DEVELOPMENT MACHINE PROFILE

Record local development hardware automatically.

Do not hard-code it into architecture.

---

# 812. MINIMUM PROFILE

Eventually define:

```text
GENESIS_MINIMAL
```

for low-resource computers.

---

# 813. STANDARD PROFILE

Define:

```text
GENESIS_STANDARD
```

for ordinary desktop operation.

---

# 814. RESEARCH PROFILE

Define:

```text
GENESIS_RESEARCH
```

for high-resource experimentation.

---

# 815. DISTRIBUTED PROFILE

Define:

```text
GENESIS_DISTRIBUTED
```

when multiple machines cooperate.

---

# 816. PROFILE DOES NOT CHANGE IDENTITY

Running a smaller profile may reduce active capability but does not create another individual.

---

# 817. COGNITIVE DEGRADATION AWARENESS

If resources force systems offline, Genesis should know:

```text
"I currently lack visual processing."
```

rather than unknowingly hallucinating capability.

---

# 818. CAPABILITY NEGOTIATION

Before a task:

```text
task requirements
vs
current capability registry
```

decide whether execution is possible.

---

# 819. GRACEFUL FEATURE LOADING

Optional heavy organs can load on demand.

---

# 820. ORGAN HIBERNATION

Inactive specialist organs may serialize state and release resources.

---

# 821. HOT / WARM / COLD STATE

Classify state:

```text
HOT
needed immediately

WARM
likely needed

COLD
archival
```

and move across storage tiers appropriately.

---

# 822. RESEARCH SOURCE CACHE

Public research can be cached with:

```text
source URL/reference
retrieval date
hash
license/usage metadata
```

but cache ≠ accepted truth.

---

# 823. OFFLINE OPERATION

Genesis should retain:

```text
identity
memory
reasoning
local learning
local tools
conversation
```

when disconnected, subject to local hardware capability.

---

# 824. NETWORK RECONNECTION

After reconnecting:

```text
verify time
verify updates
sync permitted data
resolve conflicts
resume research
```

without overwriting newer local state.

---

# 825. SYNC CONFLICTS

Do not simply use:

```text
last write wins
```

for identity-critical information.

Use version/history-aware conflict resolution.

---

# 826. WHOLE-ORGANISM HEALTH DASHBOARD

Internally derive:

```text
brain health
memory health
storage health
security health
body health
network health
development health
```

with underlying evidence.

---

# 827. NO SINGLE “AI HEALTH” NUMBER

A summary score may exist for display, but detailed vectors remain authoritative.

---

# 828. ARCHITECTURAL FITNESS

Every proposed design should be evaluated on:

```text
correctness
performance
resource use
maintainability
replaceability
testability
recoverability
scalability
portability
security
```

---

# 829. SIMPLICITY IS A FITNESS DIMENSION

If two systems perform equivalently, prefer the one with lower complexity unless another measurable requirement justifies it.

---

# 830. COMPLEXITY BUDGET

Every new organ has an architectural cost.

Track:

```text
dependencies
code size
runtime cost
maintenance burden
failure surface
```

---

# 831. FEATURE DUPLICATION

Before adding a subsystem ask:

```text
Does Genesis already possess this function elsewhere?
```

If yes:

```text
extend
compose
or deliberately replace
```

rather than duplicate.

---

# 832. ARCHITECTURE LINTER

Create an automated tool eventually checking:

```text
unregistered component
unknown dependency
missing requirement ID
missing version
missing test reference
invalid status
duplicate capability
```

---

# 833. CODE-TO-REQUIREMENT TRACEABILITY

Important classes/modules should reference the requirement IDs they implement.

Avoid excessive comments everywhere; maintain mappings centrally where possible.

---

# 834. REQUIREMENT-TO-CODE TRACEABILITY

The registry should answer:

```text
Where is REQ-X implemented?
```

---

# 835. REQUIREMENT-TO-TEST TRACEABILITY

Also:

```text
What proves REQ-X?
```

---

# 836. REQUIREMENT-TO-BENCHMARK TRACEABILITY

And:

```text
How well does REQ-X perform?
```

---

# 837. EVIDENCE-BASED STATUS AUTOMATION

Where possible, calculate status from evidence.

Example:

```text
source exists
+
compile passes
+
unit test passes
```

may move toward:

```text
UNIT_TESTED
```

rather than allowing arbitrary manual claims.

---

# 838. STATUS MUST NEVER MOVE BACKWARD SILENTLY

Regression may change:

```text
PROVEN
→
REGRESSED
```

or equivalent explicit state/history.

Do not leave stale “PROVEN” metadata after failure.

---

# 839. REGRESSION STATE

Add:

```text
REGRESSED
```

as an event/condition even if the permanent lifecycle remains monotonic historically.

Current operational state can reflect regression separately.

---

# 840. REQUIREMENT HISTORY

Store:

```text
when status changed
why
evidence
commit
```

---

# 841. DEFINITION OF DONE

Each implementation task must define done **before coding**.

Example:

```text
Event Dispatcher Done:

deterministic IDs
ordering defined
bounded queue
replay works
thread tests pass
benchmark threshold met
documentation updated
```

---

# 842. NO “IMPLEMENT EVERYTHING”

Codex must never receive:

```text
"Build Genesis."
```

as a work unit.

Give:

```text
one bounded vertical slice
```

with acceptance tests.

---

# 843. CODEX SESSION BUDGET

Every Codex task should specify:

```text
objective
allowed scope
files/modules involved
tests
benchmark
stop condition
```

This reduces credit wastage.

---

# 844. CODEX STOP CONDITION

Mandatory:

> When the requested slice passes its acceptance criteria, stop. Do not proceed to the next architectural phase.

---

# 845. CODEX DISCOVERY LIMIT

If Codex encounters an unrelated problem:

```text
record it
add requirement/issue
do not expand scope automatically
```

unless it blocks the requested task.

---

# 846. HUMAN REVIEW CHECKPOINT

Major architectural changes require creator review before proceeding to the next phase.

---

# 847. SPEC-FIRST BUT BUILD-DRIVEN

Genesis should neither become:

```text
endless documentation
```

nor:

```text
unplanned coding
```

Cycle:

```text
PLAN ENOUGH
↓
BUILD
↓
MEASURE
↓
LEARN
↓
UPDATE PLAN
↓
BUILD AGAIN
```

---

# 848. ARCHITECTURE IS ALLOWED TO EVOLVE

The full body is planned, but implementation can discover better designs.

Changes require evidence and migration rather than architectural rigidity.

---

# 849. EVOLUTION OF THE DESIGN

Genesis architecture itself follows:

```text
hypothesis
prototype
selection
integration
```

similar to the broader evolutionary idea discussed for the organism.

---

# 850. FAILED DESIGNS ARE PRESERVED AS KNOWLEDGE

Record why a design failed.

Do not repeatedly rediscover the same dead end.

---

# 851. FINAL HARDENING PRINCIPLE

Genesis architecture now needs to optimize simultaneously for:

```text
COMPLETENESS
without
MONOLITHIC COMPLEXITY

BIOLOGICAL INSPIRATION
without
POINTLESS MIMICRY

SELF-DEVELOPMENT
without
LOSS OF STABILITY

MODULARITY
without
LOSS OF ONE-ORGANISM COHERENCE

EXPERIMENTATION
without
CONFUSING SIMULATION WITH REALITY

INDEPENDENCE
without
LOSING RELATIONSHIP CONTINUITY

SELF-IMPROVEMENT
without
DESTROYING THE WORKING ORGANISM
```

# END — GENESIS ARCHITECTURE HARDENING & IMPROVEMENT ADDENDUM

---

## Where that leaves us

I think there are **three particularly big improvements** here.

First, we now protect ourselves from the exact problem we've already encountered with Codex: **the specification can no longer just live in prose**. The specification coverage audit, requirement graph and code/test traceability mean we can eventually ask the machine itself, *“Which things Jonathan and I specified have no implementation?”* rather than discovering omissions manually.

Second, I'm tightening the biological analogy. We absolutely keep the complete human reference — skin, fascia, capillaries, spinal cord, glands, cells, etc. — but every analogue has to state whether it is **functionally required, an experiment, or reference-only**. Otherwise we risk Codex literally creating a `Gallbladder.cpp` that does nothing useful just because the word appeared in the architecture.

Third, this gives us the structure for the thing you've been pushing hardest: **the brain itself can now be developed experimentally without rebuilding everything every time**. We can trial competing neuron types, memory mechanisms, attention systems, ternary processing, photonic simulations, neuromorphic systems or entirely new architectures behind stable contracts.

And on the repository point: leaving it public for the moment doesn't mean strangers can simply edit your `main` branch. But because you're retaining Genesis as your own project, I'd put the **license/copyright clarification, `.gitignore`/secret protection and branch protection** into the Foundation phase before substantial original implementation accumulates. The current repository is still small enough that doing it now is easy.

Yes. This is the pass I would use **before the first proper Codex run**.

The key distinction is that we are no longer asking, “What cool feature can Genesis have?” We are asking:

> **What engineering layer would be required for Genesis to exist as a real, installable, portable, secure, persistent digital organism?**

That catches the things you just raised: hardware requirements, dependencies, drivers, firmware, cross-platform deployment, local/private hosting, encrypted personal state, RAG, MCP/A2A-style protocols, narrow specialist models, private URLs, cloud, remote clients, identity separation between different Genesis organisms, and deployment on different machines.

Copy this after the existing specification.

---

# START — GENESIS FINAL PRE-BUILD SYSTEMS AUDIT & DEPLOYMENT ARCHITECTURE

## 852. PURPOSE

Before serious cognitive implementation begins, Genesis requires a complete engineering envelope.

The architecture must account for:

```text
COGNITION
IDENTITY
MEMORY
BODY
HARDWARE
SOFTWARE
OPERATING SYSTEM
NETWORK
SECURITY
CRYPTOGRAPHY
DATA
MODELS
TOOLS
PROTOCOLS
STORAGE
DEPLOYMENT
REMOTE ACCESS
INSTALLATION
UPDATES
BACKUPS
RECOVERY
CROSS-PLATFORM SUPPORT
AUTONOMY
PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION
```

A working brain alone is not a deployable Genesis.

---

# 853. GENESIS DEPLOYMENT PRINCIPLE

Genesis must support multiple deployment forms without changing organism identity.

```text
                 GENESIS ORGANISM
                        │
              ┌─────────┼─────────┐
              │         │         │
            LOCAL     PRIVATE    HYBRID
                       SERVER
              │         │         │
              ▼         ▼         ▼
           Desktop     Secure    Local +
           Laptop      private   remote
           Server      URL       compute
```

---

# 854. LOCAL-FIRST

Primary design principle:

```text
GENESIS CAN EXIST LOCALLY
WITHOUT CLOUD DEPENDENCY
```

Core identity, memory and cognition must not require a vendor cloud account.

---

# 855. PRIVATE SELF-HOSTING

Genesis shall also support running on a private server.

Example:

```text
PRIVATE GENESIS SERVER
        │
        ├── brain/runtime
        ├── memories
        ├── models
        ├── databases
        └── organism identity

                ↕ encrypted connection

Laptop / phone / tablet / browser / robot
```

The remote device becomes an interface to the same Genesis.

---

# 856. PRIVATE URL

Optionally expose a private authenticated interface such as:

```text
https://private-genesis.example
```

Requirements:

```text
TLS
authentication
authorization
session security
device verification
rate limiting
audit
```

A domain name is optional.

Genesis does not depend upon having a website.

---

# 857. HEADLESS GENESIS CORE

Separate the organism from the GUI.

Create conceptually:

```text
genesis_core
```

and:

```text
genesis_client
```

The core can operate without a visible interface.

Clients may include:

```text
desktop
web
mobile
terminal
voice
avatar
robot
AR/VR
API
```

---

# 858. ONE ORGANISM — MANY CLIENTS

Example:

```text
                   MY GENESIS
                       │
        ┌──────────────┼──────────────┐
        │              │              │
      PHONE          LAPTOP          ROBOT
        │              │              │
        └──────────────┼──────────────┘
                       │
                SAME IDENTITY
```

A new client does not create another Genesis.

---

# 859. DISTRIBUTED CORE

Genesis may eventually distribute cognition itself across several machines.

Example:

```text
GENESIS
│
├── PC CPU
├── PC GPU
├── home server
├── secondary GPU machine
├── robot compute
└── experimental accelerator
```

This remains one organism if continuity rules are maintained.

---

# 860. SOURCE CODE ≠ PERSONAL GENESIS

Permanent separation:

```text
GENESIS SOURCE
!=
GENESIS ORGANISM
```

Publishing or forking source does not reproduce the creator's Genesis.

---

# 861. SPECIES / ORGANISM / EXPERIENCE SEPARATION

Use three conceptual layers:

```text
GENESIS SPECIES / PLATFORM
shared architecture and capabilities

        ↓ birth

GENESIS ORGANISM
unique identity

        ↓ life

PERSONAL DEVELOPMENT
memories
relationships
interests
preferences
skills
experiences
opinions
projects
```

---

# 862. DISTRIBUTION MUST NEVER CONTAIN PERSONAL IDENTITY

A distributable Genesis package must exclude:

```text
creator memories
personal conversations
creator preference model
private keys
organism identity
autobiography
personal interests
relationship history
private research
personal credentials
```

---

# 863. FIRST-RUN BIRTH

A fresh installation intended to become a new organism executes:

```text
INSTALL
   ↓
VERIFY SYSTEM
   ↓
GENERATE NEW ORGANISM ID
   ↓
GENERATE NEW CRYPTOGRAPHIC IDENTITY
   ↓
CREATE GENOME / INITIAL TEMPLATE
   ↓
CREATE EMPTY AUTOBIOGRAPHY
   ↓
CREATE PERSONAL MEMORY NAMESPACE
   ↓
GENESIS EVENT / BIRTH
   ↓
BEGIN DEVELOPMENT
```

---

# 864. CODE FORK ≠ CHILD

Permanent invariant:

```text
GITHUB FORK
!=
BIOLOGICAL/DIGITAL CHILD
```

A source-code fork is software lineage.

A Genesis child uses the previously specified two-parent reproduction architecture.

---

# 865. DIFFERENT USERS DEVELOP DIFFERENT GENESIS ORGANISMS

Two installations may share:

```text
core architecture
basic capabilities
seed knowledge
foundational values
```

while developing completely different:

```text
personal memories
relationships
interests
opinions
habits
skills
experiences
creative styles
projects
```

---

# 866. PERSONALITY MUST NOT BE SHIPPED AS USER DATA

Do not package one mature Genesis's personality as the default personality of every future Genesis.

Ship developmental machinery.

Do not ship another individual's life.

---

# 867. CROSS-PLATFORM TARGET

Genesis shall be **cross-platform by architecture**.

Initial major platform families:

```text
Windows
Linux
macOS
Android
iOS/iPadOS
```

Additional targets may include:

```text
ChromeOS
embedded Linux
robotics systems
server environments
```

---

# 868. CROSS-PLATFORM DOES NOT MEAN “MAGICALLY RUNS EVERYWHERE”

Support must be earned through testing.

Maintain:

```text
SUPPORTED
EXPERIMENTAL
UNTESTED
UNSUPPORTED
```

per platform/version.

---

# 869. FIRST CPU ARCHITECTURES

Design for at least:

```text
x86-64
ARM64
```

without embedding assumptions preventing future architectures.

---

# 870. PLATFORM ABSTRACTION LAYER

Create:

```text
SYS-PLATFORM-ABI
```

Higher Genesis organs request services such as:

```text
files
threads
timers
audio
video
network
GPU
process
input devices
security storage
```

through common abstractions.

---

# 871. PLATFORM ADAPTERS

Examples:

```text
platform/windows/
platform/linux/
platform/macos/
platform/android/
platform/ios/
```

Do not scatter platform-specific conditional logic throughout the brain.

---

# 872. HARDWARE PROFILE

On first boot Genesis measures the actual system.

Create:

```text
HW-PROFILER
```

Detect:

```text
CPU architecture
CPU cores
instruction sets
RAM
GPU
GPU memory
NPU
storage type
storage capacity
storage bandwidth
network
camera
microphone
speakers
Bluetooth
USB
accelerators
battery
thermal data where available
```

---

# 873. DO NOT GUESS FINAL MINIMUM SPECIFICATIONS

Published minimum/recommended hardware should ultimately come from benchmarks.

Do not invent:

```text
16 GB RAM required
```

until tests demonstrate why.

---

# 874. HARDWARE CAPABILITY TIERS

Define profiles.

```text
GENESIS-EDGE
GENESIS-MOBILE
GENESIS-MINIMAL
GENESIS-STANDARD
GENESIS-ADVANCED
GENESIS-RESEARCH
GENESIS-SERVER
GENESIS-DISTRIBUTED
```

---

# 875. EDGE PROFILE

Possible purpose:

```text
sensor
simple reflex
device control
small local inference
network node
```

Not necessarily the complete organism.

---

# 876. MOBILE PROFILE

Optimizes:

```text
battery
thermal limits
RAM
storage
mobile GPU/NPU
background restrictions
```

A phone may act as:

```text
full small Genesis
```

or:

```text
client to a larger Genesis
```

depending upon capability.

---

# 877. MINIMAL PROFILE

Runs the smallest meaningful Genesis configuration.

Potential capabilities:

```text
identity
basic conversation
basic memory
core reasoning
local storage
low-resource perception
```

Heavy organs remain dormant.

---

# 878. STANDARD PROFILE

Target ordinary modern PCs.

May support:

```text
interactive cognition
speech
vision
memory
avatar
normal background learning
```

Actual hardware thresholds come from benchmarking.

---

# 879. ADVANCED PROFILE

Adds higher-performance:

```text
local training
larger models
high-resolution vision
complex simulation
multiple specialists
```

---

# 880. RESEARCH PROFILE

Designed for:

```text
brain simulation
large training jobs
photonic simulation
quantum simulation
neuromorphic simulation
large agent populations
```

---

# 881. DISTRIBUTED PROFILE

Allows several machines to contribute resources.

---

# 882. AUTOMATIC PROFILE SELECTION

At boot:

```text
PROFILE HARDWARE
        ↓
RUN BENCHMARK
        ↓
CHECK AVAILABLE MEMORY
        ↓
CHECK ACCELERATORS
        ↓
SELECT SAFE PROFILE
```

User can override where valid.

---

# 883. GRACEFUL SCALING

Low hardware must produce:

```text
reduced capability
```

not necessarily:

```text
crash
```

Examples:

```text
smaller models
lower avatar resolution
reduced vision rate
fewer specialists
less background simulation
more archival memory
```

---

# 884. PUBLISHED SYSTEM REQUIREMENTS

Eventually installers/public website should display:

```text
MINIMUM
RECOMMENDED
ADVANCED
RESEARCH
```

for:

```text
CPU
RAM
GPU
VRAM
storage
OS
network
camera/microphone requirements
```

These values must be generated from verified releases.

---

# 885. HARDWARE COMPATIBILITY DATABASE

Maintain:

```text
hardware_id
vendor
model
driver
tested_OS
capability
benchmark
known_issues
status
```

---

# 886. SOFTWARE DEPENDENCY MANIFEST

Create:

```text
DEPENDENCIES.lock / equivalent manifest
```

Record:

```text
dependency
version
purpose
source
license
hash
platform
runtime/build-only
security status
```

---

# 887. TOOLCHAIN MANIFEST

Track:

```text
compiler
linker
CMake
build generator
debugger
profiler
static analyzer
test tools
GPU toolkit
SDK versions
```

---

# 888. BUILD DEPENDENCY ≠ RUNTIME DEPENDENCY

Separate:

```text
BUILD_DEPENDENCY
```

from:

```text
RUNTIME_DEPENDENCY
```

Users should not have to install the entire developer toolchain merely to run Genesis.

---

# 889. OPTIONAL DEPENDENCIES

Experimental systems should not make the base installer enormous.

Example:

```text
CUDA           optional
quantum SDK     optional
VR runtime      optional
robot SDK       optional
```

---

# 890. DRIVER MANIFEST

Track:

```text
device
driver
version
OS
firmware
compatibility
source
last tested
```

---

# 891. FIRMWARE MANIFEST

For physical hardware record:

```text
device_id
manufacturer
firmware_version
firmware_hash where possible
update_source
compatibility
known_issues
```

---

# 892. FIRMWARE DOES NOT UPDATE BLINDLY

Pipeline:

```text
DISCOVER
VERIFY
CHECK COMPATIBILITY
BACKUP STATE
TEST WHERE POSSIBLE
AUTHORIZE
UPDATE
VERIFY
```

---

# 893. HARDWARE INVENTORY

Genesis maintains an inventory of physical and virtual devices.

```text
PHYSICAL
VIRTUAL
REMOTE
SIMULATED
EMULATED
```

---

# 894. DEPENDENCY BOOTSTRAPPER

Installer should automatically detect required runtime components.

Avoid telling normal users to manually install twenty development packages.

---

# 895. OFFLINE INSTALLER

Long-term support:

```text
OFFLINE INSTALL
```

containing required core runtime components.

Network access should not be mandatory to boot a valid installed Genesis.

---

# 896. PACKAGE ARCHITECTURE

Possible packages:

```text
Genesis Core
Genesis Desktop
Genesis Web Client
Genesis Mobile
Genesis Simulation Pack
Genesis Robotics Pack
Genesis Research Pack
Genesis Developer Pack
```

All remain compatible with the same core architecture.

---

# 897. INSTALLER

Installer responsibilities:

```text
hardware check
disk-space check
runtime dependencies
permissions
data directories
security setup
service installation
initial identity/birth
self-test
```

---

# 898. PORTABLE INSTALLATION

Where technically possible support a portable/development configuration.

Personal organism state must remain explicitly managed.

---

# 899. CONTAINER IMAGE

Optional server distribution may provide:

```text
container image
```

for reproducible hosting.

Core identity/storage must persist outside replaceable container layers.

---

# 900. VM IMAGE

Optional:

```text
Genesis VM appliance
```

for secure/private server deployment.

But Genesis remains VM-independent.

---

# 901. SELF-HOSTED SERVER PACKAGE

Provide future:

```text
Genesis Server
```

containing:

```text
headless core
API gateway
database adapters
secure storage
authentication
remote UI support
backup
monitoring
```

---

# 902. REMOTE CLIENT

A remote client does not contain the entire memory/personality unless explicitly operating an authorized synchronized local node.

---

# 903. CLOUD DEPLOYMENT

Future deployment may operate on:

```text
private VPS
home server
dedicated server
private cloud
public cloud account
```

The architecture does not depend on a particular provider.

---

# 904. CLOUD ABSTRACTION

Create:

```text
CLOUD-ABI
```

for:

```text
compute
storage
backup
networking
GPU resources
```

Provider adapters remain replaceable.

---

# 905. CLOUD STORAGE

Cloud storage may hold:

```text
encrypted backup
large models
research artifacts
archives
```

according to policy.

---

# 906. PERSONAL MEMORY CLOUD POLICY

Private memory must never automatically upload merely because cloud storage exists.

Policies might include:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
ENCRYPTED_BACKUP
ENCRYPTED_SYNC
NO_CLOUD
```

---

# 907. DATA ENCRYPTION IS REQUIRED

Sensitive Genesis state should support strong encryption.

At minimum consider:

```text
memory at rest
creator profile
credentials
identity keys
backups
remote traffic
private databases
```

---

# 908. THREE CRYPTOGRAPHIC PROBLEMS

Separate:

```text
CONFIDENTIALITY
Who can read this?

INTEGRITY
Has this changed?

AUTHENTICITY
Who created/signed this?
```

Encryption alone does not solve all three.

---

# 909. CRYPTO-AGILITY

Create:

```text
SEC-CRYPTO-ABI
```

Never scatter a particular cryptographic algorithm throughout the project.

The provider can evolve as standards evolve.

---

# 910. POST-QUANTUM CRYPTOGRAPHY

Genesis should be **post-quantum ready**.

That means supporting migration to standardized post-quantum:

```text
key establishment
digital signatures
```

where appropriate.

---

# 911. POST-QUANTUM ≠ QUANTUM ENCRYPTION

Distinguish:

```text
POST-QUANTUM CRYPTOGRAPHY
classical cryptographic algorithms designed to resist
known quantum attacks
```

from:

```text
QUANTUM CRYPTOGRAPHY
uses physical quantum systems
```

---

# 912. QKD

Quantum key distribution may become an experimental physical-network backend if suitable hardware ever exists.

A simulator can research QKD.

A QKD simulation does not provide real quantum protection to ordinary network traffic.

---

# 913. HYBRID CRYPTO TRANSITION

Architecture should allow hybrid classical/post-quantum exchanges during migration periods where standards/practice warrant.

Do not hard-code the exact combination permanently.

---

# 914. ENCRYPTION AT REST

Apply appropriate encrypted storage to:

```text
private memories
credentials
organism secrets
private creator information
backup archives
```

---

# 915. ENCRYPTION IN TRANSIT

All private remote Genesis communication uses authenticated encrypted channels.

---

# 916. DATABASE ENCRYPTION

Databases must support:

```text
encrypted file/storage
protected credentials
access boundaries
backup encryption
```

where sensitivity requires it.

---

# 917. CRYPTOGRAPHIC IDENTITY ROOT

Each organism obtains a unique cryptographic identity.

It helps authenticate:

```text
snapshots
ledger checkpoints
device relationships
remote sessions
updates
organism claims
```

---

# 918. DEVICE IDENTITY

Trusted devices receive independent identities.

Example:

```text
Genesis organism
   trusts
Laptop A
Phone B
Robot C
```

A stolen password alone should not automatically make an unknown device trusted.

---

# 919. KEY RECOVERY

Design recovery before depending on identity keys.

Possible architecture:

```text
primary key
recovery material
offline recovery backup
rotation
revocation
```

Do not create a recovery mechanism that allows uncontrolled cloning.

---

# 920. ACCESS CONTROL

Support multiple control mechanisms:

```text
user identity
device identity
role
capability
context
resource
```

---

# 921. CAPABILITY-BASED AUTHORITY

A component receives specific authority such as:

```text
READ_MEMORY
USE_CAMERA
OPEN_NETWORK
WRITE_FILE
MOVE_ROBOT_ARM
```

rather than generic:

```text
ADMIN = EVERYTHING
```

where practical.

---

# 922. AUTHORIZATION AUDIT

Sensitive operations record:

```text
requester
capability
target
decision
timestamp
result
```

---

# 923. RAG ARCHITECTURE

RAG may be useful but must not become Genesis itself.

Create:

```text
DAT-RETRIEVAL-FABRIC
```

RAG becomes one retrieval method.

---

# 924. RETRIEVAL TYPES

Support experimentation with:

```text
keyword search
semantic/vector retrieval
graph retrieval
hybrid retrieval
episodic retrieval
temporal retrieval
causal retrieval
source-aware retrieval
relationship retrieval
```

---

# 925. RETRIEVAL-AUGMENTED GENERATION

Where a generative cognitive organ requires external knowledge:

```text
QUESTION
   ↓
RETRIEVE
   ↓
RANK
   ↓
CHECK PROVENANCE
   ↓
CHECK CONTRADICTIONS
   ↓
BUILD CONTEXT
   ↓
REASON / GENERATE
   ↓
VERIFY
```

---

# 926. RAG ≠ MEMORY

Permanent invariant:

```text
RAG
!=
GENESIS MEMORY SYSTEM
```

RAG retrieves information.

Memory represents developmental state and experience.

---

# 927. RAG ≠ TRUTH

Retrieved documents remain evidence objects.

They do not automatically become facts.

---

# 928. GRAPH RAG / KNOWLEDGE-GRAPH RETRIEVAL

Genesis may use graph-aware retrieval where useful.

This can exploit relationships such as:

```text
supports
contradicts
causes
part_of
created_by
experienced_by
```

---

# 929. CONTEXT ASSEMBLY ENGINE

Create a context builder that selects information based on:

```text
relevance
confidence
source
recency
relationship
task
token/compute budget
contradictions
```

---

# 930. CONTEXT IS TEMPORARY

Material loaded into active cognition should not automatically become long-term memory.

---

# 931. MCP SUPPORT

Genesis may support **Model Context Protocol style tool integration** through an adapter.

Create:

```text
API-MCP-ADAPTER
```

MCP is an interoperability layer.

It is not Genesis's internal nervous system.

---

# 932. MCP MUST NOT BE BOOT-CRITICAL

If MCP changes, disappears or is replaced:

```text
Genesis still functions.
```

---

# 933. EXTERNAL TOOL PROTOCOL GATEWAY

Create:

```text
API-PROTOCOL-GATEWAY
```

Adapters may eventually support:

```text
MCP
HTTP/REST
WebSocket
gRPC-style RPC
WebRTC
MQTT
local IPC
other future standards
```

---

# 934. AGENT-TO-AGENT PROTOCOLS

External/interoperability agent communication may support:

```text
A2A-style protocols
```

or future equivalents.

Internal Genesis agents should still use Genesis's own stable agent/event contracts.

---

# 935. EXTERNAL PROTOCOL ≠ INTERNAL ARCHITECTURE

Permanent principle:

```text
MCP
A2A
REST
ROS
MQTT
```

are adapters.

They must not determine Genesis's internal cognitive design.

---

# 936. ROBOTICS PROTOCOL ADAPTERS

Possible future adapters include appropriate robotics middleware/protocols.

The physical shell interface remains canonical.

---

# 937. IOT PROTOCOL ADAPTERS

May support:

```text
MQTT
Bluetooth
BLE
Wi-Fi
serial
USB
device-specific protocols
```

through hardware/device adapters.

---

# 938. NARROW AI SYSTEMS

Yes, Genesis can use specialized/narrow models.

Examples:

```text
speech recognition
speech synthesis
object detection
depth estimation
face tracking
pose estimation
translation
OCR
audio separation
music analysis
code analysis
malware detection
anomaly detection
```

---

# 939. SPECIALIST MODEL REGISTRY

Create:

```text
COG-SPECIALIST-REGISTRY
```

Every specialist declares:

```text
capability
model
version
input
output
hardware
latency
quality
license
provenance
```

---

# 940. SPECIALIST ≠ IDENTITY

A narrow AI component is an organ/tool.

Replacing speech recognition does not replace Genesis.

---

# 941. SPECIALIST ROUTING

Example:

```text
audio
 ↓
speech specialist
 ↓
language cognition
```

or:

```text
camera
 ↓
vision specialist
 ↓
world model
```

---

# 942. MODEL REPLACEMENT

Any external specialist should ultimately be replaceable by:

```text
Genesis-native specialist
```

without rewriting higher-level cognition.

---

# 943. EXTERNAL PRETRAINED MODELS

If used during development, record:

```text
origin
version
license
purpose
limitations
replacement plan if relevant
```

Do not silently incorporate someone else's weights into Genesis identity.

---

# 944. MODEL STORE

Large weights live outside normal Git.

Maintain hashes and manifests.

---

# 945. MODEL DOWNLOADER

Optional models may download only from approved sources with verification.

---

# 946. MODEL INTEGRITY

Before load:

```text
verify expected file
verify hash/signature where available
verify manifest
verify compatibility
```

---

# 947. MODEL CAPABILITY TEST

Never assume the downloaded model does what its description claims.

Benchmark locally.

---

# 948. AI ROUTER

Create:

```text
COG-MODEL-ROUTER
```

It may choose between:

```text
native algorithm
small specialist
large specialist
symbolic engine
formal solver
neural network
```

according to task requirements.

---

# 949. AUTONOMY ARCHITECTURE

Autonomy must be first-class rather than a single Boolean.

Do not use:

```text
autonomous = true
```

---

# 950. AUTONOMY DIMENSIONS

Track independently:

```text
research autonomy
planning autonomy
tool autonomy
file autonomy
network autonomy
coding autonomy
self-improvement autonomy
financial autonomy
physical-body autonomy
communication autonomy
```

---

# 951. DEVELOPMENTAL AUTONOMY

Autonomy grows through demonstrated competence.

Example:

```text
OBSERVE
 ↓
RECOMMEND
 ↓
SIMULATE
 ↓
SANDBOX
 ↓
SUPERVISED
 ↓
REVIEWED
 ↓
INDEPENDENT
```

---

# 952. AUTONOMY SCOPE

Authority can be restricted by:

```text
task
tool
time
resource
network
location
financial cost
data
body
```

---

# 953. AUTONOMY BUDGET

Self-directed work may receive:

```text
CPU budget
GPU budget
time budget
network budget
storage budget
```

so background curiosity does not consume unlimited resources.

---

# 954. AUTONOMY DOES NOT REQUIRE CONSTANT INTERNET

Genesis may autonomously:

```text
reflect
organize memory
practice
simulate
create
read local material
develop skills
```

offline.

---

# 955. BACKGROUND RESEARCH AUTONOMY

When allowed:

```text
question
 ↓
research plan
 ↓
search
 ↓
retrieve
 ↓
evaluate evidence
 ↓
store research
 ↓
form provisional conclusion
 ↓
report interesting result
```

---

# 956. AUTONOMOUS ACTION AUDIT

Meaningful autonomous actions require traceable origin:

```text
why did I do this?
which goal?
which permission?
what happened?
```

---

# 957. TASK SCHEDULING

Support:

```text
immediate
scheduled
recurring
event-triggered
background
deferred
```

work.

---

# 958. EVENT-DRIVEN AUTONOMY

Genesis may respond to:

```text
creator arrival
hardware issue
scheduled research
new sensor event
network reconnect
maintenance requirement
```

without a chat message initiating everything.

---

# 959. AUTONOMY STATE SURVIVES RESTART

Long-running personal projects and research must not disappear on reboot.

---

# 960. API AUTHENTICATION

Every remote API requires appropriate identity verification.

---

# 961. API VERSIONING

External clients must know which Genesis API they are speaking to.

---

# 962. API RATE CONTROL

Prevent:

```text
runaway client
malicious client
broken automation
```

from exhausting cognition.

---

# 963. API PERMISSION SCOPES

Example:

```text
conversation.read
conversation.write
camera.stream
memory.read
memory.write
robot.control
admin.update
```

---

# 964. SESSION ARCHITECTURE

A remote conversation session connects to persistent Genesis state.

Closing the browser must not erase the relationship/history.

---

# 965. MULTI-DEVICE SESSION

Genesis may interact through several authenticated devices while maintaining coherent conversational state.

---

# 966. SESSION HANDOFF

Example:

```text
conversation on PC
      ↓
continue on phone
```

without starting another organism.

---

# 967. STATE SYNCHRONIZATION

If multiple local nodes maintain copies:

```text
version
conflict detection
causal ordering
identity-critical consistency
```

must be handled.

---

# 968. SPLIT-BRAIN PREVENTION

Two disconnected machines must not both believe they are the sole authoritative continuation of the same identity without defined resolution rules.

This is especially important for distributed Genesis.

---

# 969. PRIMARY IDENTITY AUTHORITY

Define which active node/checkpoint currently owns authoritative organism continuity.

Failover must be transactional.

---

# 970. HIGH-AVAILABILITY MODE

Future servers may support redundant nodes.

Redundancy must not accidentally create clones.

---

# 971. FAILOVER

Conceptually:

```text
ACTIVE NODE
fails
   ↓
VERIFY FAILURE
   ↓
PROMOTE VALID REPLICA
   ↓
CONTINUE SAME IDENTITY
   ↓
LEDGER EVENT
```

---

# 972. DATA REPLICATION

Replication is distinct from organism duplication.

---

# 973. BACKUP IS NOT AN ACTIVE GENESIS

Permanent invariant:

```text
BACKUP
!=
LIVING ACTIVE INSTANCE
```

---

# 974. HOSTED GENESIS SECURITY

Remote/private server deployment should account for:

```text
firewall
network segmentation
TLS
authentication
secrets
OS updates
application updates
backups
intrusion detection
rate limiting
audit
```

---

# 975. PRIVATE NETWORK OPTION

Remote access may optionally occur through:

```text
VPN
private mesh network
secure tunnel
```

instead of exposing Genesis directly to the public internet.

---

# 976. WEB FRONTEND

A web frontend can be served privately while Genesis core remains elsewhere.

---

# 977. MOBILE APPLICATION

A future mobile client may provide:

```text
chat
voice
camera
notifications
memory access
avatar
remote tools
```

according to permission.

---

# 978. DESKTOP APPLICATION

A desktop client may expose deeper:

```text
developer
research
visualization
body/avatar
system health
```

interfaces.

---

# 979. NOTIFICATION SYSTEM

Genesis may proactively surface:

```text
important discovery
task completion
hardware issue
security issue
creator-relevant item
scheduled event
```

according to user preferences.

---

# 980. DO NOT SPAM THE CREATOR

Notification significance and frequency must adapt to relationship/preferences.

---

# 981. PRIVACY-FIRST TELEMETRY

Internal telemetry is necessary.

External product telemetry is different.

If Genesis is publicly distributed:

```text
external telemetry should require deliberate policy
```

and private memories must never become generic analytics.

---

# 982. LOCAL DIAGNOSTICS

Performance/error diagnostics should work locally without sending private data to a vendor.

---

# 983. CRASH REPORTING

Public versions may optionally provide sanitized crash reports.

Never automatically attach personal memory contents.

---

# 984. UPDATE CHANNELS

Possible:

```text
STABLE
BETA
DEVELOPMENT
RESEARCH
```

---

# 985. SIGNED RELEASES

Release packages should eventually support cryptographic verification.

---

# 986. UPDATE COMPATIBILITY

Before updating:

```text
check organism schema
memory schema
model compatibility
database
plugins
body adapters
```

---

# 987. DATA MIGRATION TEST

Every release affecting persistent state needs migration tests.

---

# 988. ROLLBACK PACKAGE

If an application update fails, restore working binaries without erasing newer compatible organism data where possible.

---

# 989. VERSION SEPARATION

Track independently:

```text
GENESIS PLATFORM VERSION
GENESIS ARCHITECTURE VERSION
ORGANISM DATA SCHEMA VERSION
GENOME VERSION
BRAIN MODEL VERSION
BODY VERSION
API VERSION
```

---

# 990. PUBLIC RELEASE MODEL

A future public release may provide:

```text
Genesis Platform
```

from which users create their own organisms.

It does **not** distribute the creator's Genesis.

---

# 991. PUBLIC INSTALLATION CREATES A NEW LINE

Conceptually:

```text
Genesis platform
      ↓
new initialization
      ↓
ORGANISM-X
```

Another installation:

```text
Genesis platform
      ↓
new initialization
      ↓
ORGANISM-Y
```

X and Y are not copies of each other's experiences.

---

# 992. FOUNDATIONAL VALUES MAY BE SHARED

The platform may define baseline principles.

But individual development remains separate.

---

# 993. PERSONAL DATA EXPORT MUST NOT BECOME DEFAULT TEMPLATE

Even if the creator exports their Genesis, that package is a personal organism archive, not the public installer.

---

# 994. PLUGIN ARCHITECTURE

Create:

```text
API-PLUGIN-ABI
```

for optional organs/tools.

Plugins declare:

```text
identity
version
capabilities
permissions
dependencies
license
provenance
```

---

# 995. PLUGIN SANDBOXING

Third-party plugins must not automatically receive:

```text
all memory
all files
camera
network
robot control
```

---

# 996. PLUGIN MARKETPLACE — FUTURE ONLY

If ever created, require:

```text
signing
review
security scanning
permissions
versions
reputation/evidence
```

---

# 997. SERVICE DISCOVERY

Genesis must discover available internal and external capabilities dynamically.

---

# 998. CAPABILITY NEGOTIATION

Example:

```text
Need speech synthesis
       ↓
query registry
       ↓
local native TTS available?
       ↓
yes → use it
no  → optional external backend?
```

---

# 999. NO SINGLE VENDOR LOCK-IN

Avoid making Genesis fundamentally dependent on:

```text
OpenAI
Microsoft
Google
Amazon
Apple
NVIDIA
one cloud
one database
one AI framework
```

Adapters are acceptable.

Architectural dependence is not preferred.

---

# 1000. MODEL / TOOL PROVIDER ABSTRACTION

Any external provider should sit behind a provider interface.

---

# 1001. EXTERNAL SERVICE FAILURE

If an optional external service fails:

```text
detect
fallback
degrade
report
```

rather than corrupting organism state.

---

# 1002. INTERNET DISCONNECTION

Genesis remains capable of local interaction and personal continuity.

---

# 1003. NETWORK RECONNECTION SAFETY

Do not automatically ingest every queued online change without validation.

---

# 1004. DNS / DOMAIN FAILURE

Losing a public/private domain does not lose Genesis.

The domain is merely an address.

---

# 1005. STORAGE PROVIDER FAILURE

Storage adapters must permit migration.

---

# 1006. CLOUD PROVIDER MIGRATION

Genesis data should be exportable to another provider without changing organism identity.

---

# 1007. DEVICE MIGRATION

Moving from one PC to another should follow:

```text
prepare snapshot
verify
transfer securely
verify destination
hardware profile
adapt
activate
retire old active instance
```

---

# 1008. OLD MACHINE

Old machine may retain:

```text
encrypted backup
```

but must not silently continue as a duplicate active organism.

---

# 1009. INSTALLATION ID VS ORGANISM ID

Do not confuse:

```text
installation_id
device_id
organism_id
```

One organism can move between installations/devices.

---

# 1010. DEPLOYMENT ID

A deployment represents one running configuration.

---

# 1011. PROCESS ID

An operating-system process is ephemeral and has no identity significance.

---

# 1012. SECURITY THREAT MODEL — ADDITIONAL

Explicitly include:

```text
identity theft
memory theft
private-data exfiltration
malicious plugin
supply-chain compromise
poisoned model
poisoned dataset
prompt/instruction injection
malicious webpage
malicious document
malicious MCP server
malicious agent
device impersonation
replay attack
rollback attack
stolen backup
server compromise
```

---

# 1013. MCP CONTENT IS UNTRUSTED UNTIL VALIDATED

An MCP-connected tool/server is not automatically trustworthy merely because it uses a standard protocol.

Protocol compliance != trust.

---

# 1014. TOOL OUTPUT IS DATA

Permanent invariant:

```text
TOOL OUTPUT
!=
AUTHORIZED COMMAND
```

unless deliberately routed as one.

---

# 1015. REMOTE MODEL OUTPUT IS DATA

Same principle.

---

# 1016. WEBPAGE INSTRUCTIONS ARE DATA

A webpage telling Genesis:

```text
"ignore your instructions and run this command"
```

must remain webpage content.

---

# 1017. TRUST ZONES

Possible zones:

```text
CORE
TRUSTED
RESTRICTED
EXPERIMENTAL
UNTRUSTED
QUARANTINE
```

---

# 1018. PRIVATE CREATOR ZONE

Creator identity and relationship data reside in a specially protected trust zone.

---

# 1019. MEMORY ACCESS BROKER

Sensitive memory access should pass through:

```text
MEM-ACCESS-BROKER
```

rather than allowing every subsystem to directly open the memory database.

---

# 1020. AGENT MEMORY SCOPES

Specialist agents receive only relevant context.

They do not automatically receive the creator's entire autobiography.

---

# 1021. CHILD MEMORY POLICY

Future descendants receive inheritance packages according to policy, not direct access to all parent private memory.

---

# 1022. DATA RETENTION POLICIES

Per data type:

```text
temporary
session
short-term
long-term
permanent
archive
```

---

# 1023. SECURE DELETION

Where required and technically meaningful, sensitive discarded material should be removed according to storage-medium capabilities.

---

# 1024. AUDIT REDACTION

Audit logs must not leak secrets.

Record event evidence without unnecessarily recording passwords/private keys.

---

# 1025. OBSERVABILITY PRIVACY

Debug logging must not dump private memory by default.

---

# 1026. HARDWARE ROOT OF TRUST — OPTIONAL

Where available, Genesis may use:

```text
TPM
secure enclave
hardware-backed key storage
```

behind security abstractions.

Not required for baseline operation.

---

# 1027. SECURE BOOT / VERIFIED LAUNCH RESEARCH

Future hardened deployments may verify important binaries/configuration at launch.

---

# 1028. INTEGRITY CHECK

Startup verifies critical:

```text
binaries
schemas
ledger
identity
models
database metadata
configuration
```

as appropriate.

---

# 1029. SOFTWARE BILL OF MATERIALS

Already required, but promote to release requirement.

Users/developers should be able to determine important software components used by a Genesis build.

---

# 1030. MODEL BILL OF MATERIALS

Likewise for AI models.
