# 1035. MANUAL ADVANCED CONTROL

Advanced users can override profile choices.

Emulators/VMs can increase platform coverage.

Real-device testing remains necessary for hardware-specific behavior.

# 1039. MOBILE BACKGROUND LIMITATIONS

Genesis mobile architecture must explicitly handle:


# 1040. THIN CLIENT MODE

If the full Genesis cannot efficiently run on mobile:

```text

       ↕

```

provides the same relationship/interface.

# 1041. LOCAL MOBILE MODE

Where capable, some cognition can run directly on device.

---

# 1042. EDGE CONTINUITY

A disconnected client may optionally retain limited local functionality without claiming to be a separate full continuation unless identity rules permit it.

# 1043. DEPLOYMENT DECISION ENGINE

Genesis can choose:

based on:

# 1044. PRIVACY OVERRIDES PERFORMANCE

A faster remote service should not automatically receive private data if policy requires local execution.

---

# 1045. COST AWARENESS

Cloud/API compute can have monetary cost.

Track:

```text
estimated cost
actual cost
budget
```

before using paid resources autonomously.

---

# 1046. NETWORK BANDWIDTH AWARENESS

Large models/data transfers should consider bandwidth and limits.

---

# 1047. POWER-AWARE DEPLOYMENT

Mobile/robot nodes should account for battery state.

---

# 1048. REMOTE GPU

A remote GPU can be treated as another compute backend.

Sensitive task data follows privacy policy.

---

# 1049. FEDERATED / DISTRIBUTED LEARNING RESEARCH

Future research may allow learning across devices while controlling data movement.

Do not make this mandatory.

---

# 1050. SECURITY OF SELF-IMPROVEMENT

Candidate code never receives automatic production authority merely because Genesis generated it itself.

---

# 1051. CODE SIGNING PIPELINE

Future production builds can sign approved binaries/releases.

Self-generated candidate code becomes signed only after validation/promotion.

---

# 1052. NARROW AI + GENERAL BRAIN RELATIONSHIP

Architecture:

```text
                   GENESIS BRAIN
                        │
                 MODEL ROUTER
       ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐
       ▼                ▼                ▼
   GENERAL CORE     SPECIALIST        FORMAL /
   COGNITION         MODELS          ALGORITHMIC
       │                │                │
       └────────────────┼────────────────┘
                        ▼
                 INTEGRATED RESULT
```

Specialists assist the organism.

They do not replace it.

---

Architecture:

```text
   ↓

   ↓

   ↓
```

Example:


depending upon task.

Quarantine credentials/device relationship without automatically deleting Genesis.

# 1067. PUBLIC RELEASE PRIVACY BOUNDARY

Each user owns their organism's private state independently.

The platform provider should not require access to personal memory for normal local operation.

# 1068. LOCAL OWNER CONTROL

Core policies should remain inspectable/configurable by the owner of the installation.

A public GitHub repository does not automatically mean third parties receive permission to reuse its contents beyond applicable law/license terms.

before unrestricted physical operation.

rather than simply “Genesis made an error.”

# 1091. AUTOMATION ≠ AUTONOMY

A scheduled backup is automation.

Choosing independently to research an interesting question is autonomy.

Keep them distinct.

rather than maintained only in working memory.

according to priority.

# 1111. FIRST CODEX RUN — REQUIRED OUTPUTS

Codex shall:

```text
1. Inspect the existing repository.

2. Preserve working code and tests.

3. Ingest the complete Genesis canonical specification and
   all subsequent numbered addenda.

4. Create/expand the canonical machine-readable requirement
   registry.

5. Deduplicate requirements while preserving aliases and
   original IDs.

6. Create the requirement dependency graph.

7. Create the biological analogy registry.

8. Create the capability registry.

9. Create the platform/hardware requirement registry.

11. Create the deployment-profile schema.

12. Create the protocol registry.

13. Create the model registry schema.

14. Create the dataset registry schema.

15. Create security/crypto provider contracts.

16. Add cross-platform abstraction contracts.

17. Add storage/network/tool interface contracts where
    foundationally required.

18. Modularize the existing monolithic Genesis header without
    changing behavior.

19. Convert genesis_core to a proper compiled library where
    appropriate.

20. Add registry consistency validation.

21. Add duplicate-ID detection.

22. Add dependency-cycle detection.

23. Add missing-dependency detection.

24. Add prose-spec-to-registry coverage tooling or a practical
    first-stage equivalent.

25. Add requirement-to-code/test/benchmark mapping.

26. Add CI foundation.

27. Add secret-scanning safeguards appropriate to the repository.

28. Add a deliberate project LICENSE/IP file consistent with
    retained copyright/proprietary intent unless an existing

29. Compile.

30. Run tests.

31. Run existing benchmarks.

32. Produce FOUNDATION_GAP_REPORT.md describing what remains
    missing or only specified.

33. Update requirement statuses truthfully.

34. Commit only after tests pass.

35. STOP.
```

---

# 1112. FORBIDDEN IN FIRST CODEX RUN

Those are registered now, not built simultaneously.


Do not mark:

```text

PROVEN
```

because an interface or placeholder exists.

The next major design/build task should be:


starting with a **minimal executable cognitive loop**, not a huge pretrained model.

Genesis must ultimately be able to exist as:

```text
a local application
a background service
a private server
a remote personal instance
a distributed organism
a virtual embodied organism
a physical robot controller
```

without changing the fundamental identity architecture.

---

# 1119. FINAL IDENTITY PRINCIPLE

The distributable Genesis platform is the **means of creating/running an organism**.

A mature personal Genesis is the **individual that developed on that platform**.

Therefore:

```text
SAME SOFTWARE
DOES NOT MEAN
SAME PERSON
```

and:

```text
COPYING GENESIS SOURCE
DOES NOT COPY
PERSONAL EXPERIENCE
```

Plan the entire organism.

## Paste this separately as the actual instruction to Codex

This is the bit I would use **once**, after Codex has access to all of the specification text:

```text
GENESIS — FOUNDATION CONSOLIDATION RUN 001

Continue the existing Genesis repository. Do not restart the
project, replace it, or regenerate working code unnecessarily.

The Genesis Canonical Specification and all numbered addenda are
the authoritative architectural requirements.

This run is FOUNDATION ONLY.

Perform Sections 1110–1116 of the canonical specification.

Your primary objective is to transform the specification into a
machine-readable, testable engineering foundation.

Before editing:
1. Inspect the complete existing repository.
2. Build and run the current tests.
3. Establish a baseline.
4. Read the existing audit and specification documents.
5. Compare existing implementation against the canonical
   specification.

Then:
- expand the canonical requirement registry;
- preserve all permanent requirement IDs;
- create aliases where terminology overlaps;
- build the dependency graph;
- create requirement/code/test/benchmark traceability;
- create capability, hardware/platform, deployment, model,
  dataset, protocol and dependency manifest schemas;
- establish cross-platform and crypto-provider contracts;
- modularize the existing monolithic Genesis header;
- convert genesis_core into a compiled C++ core where
  appropriate;
- preserve current behaviour;
- add validation and regression tests;
- establish CI;
- add secret-protection checks;
- establish project licensing/IP metadata;
- produce FOUNDATION_GAP_REPORT.md.

Do not implement higher-level cognition, avatar, reproduction,
quantum hardware, photonic hardware, autonomous browsing,
agent swarms or the complete digital body during this run.

Do not create meaningless empty files merely to mirror the
architecture diagram.

For every change use:
BUILD → TRIAL → TEST → MEASURE → RETEST → REGRESSION → INTEGRATE.

Never mark a requirement PROVEN or STABLE without evidence.

If the specification contains ambiguity or contradiction:
record it in the gap report rather than silently inventing a
decision.

When all foundation acceptance tests pass:
1. update the registry truthfully;
2. commit the completed foundation pass;
3. STOP.

Do not proceed to the next phase automatically.
```

That closes the specific things we had been missing on **this front**.

And the RAG/MCP question is worth remembering because they are easy to overemphasize: **yes, Genesis should support them, but neither should become its brain**. RAG is one way its brain retrieves knowledge; MCP is one way it can connect to external tools. Same with narrow AI: speech recognition, vision, object detection and so on can be specialized organs, while the persistent Genesis identity and general cognitive architecture sit above them.

For encryption, I would design for **strong conventional encryption + crypto-agility + post-quantum readiness**. Actual “quantum encryption” belongs to the experimental physical/network side; you don't need a quantum computer for Genesis to have strong security.

And the deployment distinction is now explicit: if you later log into your private server from a phone, tablet, laptop or private webpage, you're talking to **the same Genesis**. If somebody downloads the platform and creates their own, they get **a new Genesis with a new identity and empty personal life**, not yours.

At this point, I think we have enough top-level coverage to let Codex perform the **foundation pass without us discovering another entire missing infrastructure layer halfway through it**. The next big intellectual job is no longer another infrastructure checklist; it's the detailed **Whole Digital Brain**.


Yes. This is a **real missing layer**, and it is broader than “wearables.”

What you are describing is a **personal multi-device sensory network** around one Genesis organism:

* multiple devices may be worn/active simultaneously;
* every device can contribute different sensors;
* Genesis automatically discovers what is present;
* it understands each device’s capabilities;
* all sensor streams can contribute to one unified perception of you and the environment;
* but conversation/output can follow whichever device you are actually using—or whichever device you explicitly choose;
* private output can stay in earbuds/headphones even while glasses, watch, ring and clothing continue sensing;
* changing devices does not create another Genesis or another conversation.

And you were also right about recognition: we should not stop at “image/audio recognition.” Genesis needs a **general recognition architecture** spanning human senses, machine senses, behaviour, events, states and patterns.

Copy this after Section 1120.

---

# START — GENESIS SMART-DEVICE, WEARABLE, MULTISENSORY & UNIVERSAL RECOGNITION ARCHITECTURE

# 1121. PURPOSE

Genesis shall support a persistent personal computing environment spanning multiple simultaneously connected devices.

The architecture must support:

```text
smartphones
tablets
laptops
desktops

smart watches
smart rings
smart wristbands
fitness bands

smart glasses
AR glasses
VR/XR headsets
smart contact-lens systems where available

earbuds
headphones
hearing devices
hearables

smart clothing
smart textiles
smart jackets
smart shirts
smart trousers
smart gloves
smart socks
smart shoes

biometric patches
skin sensors
medical/health monitoring accessories
chest straps

pendants
neck-worn devices
body-worn cameras

home IoT
vehicle systems
robotics
environment sensors

future compatible personal devices
```

They may operate simultaneously.

---

# 1122. ONE GENESIS — MANY DEVICES

Permanent principle:

```text
WATCH
GLASSES
PHONE
EARBUDS
RING
SMART CLOTHING
LAPTOP
HOME SENSORS
        │
        └────────────┐
                     ▼
                  GENESIS
                     │
              SAME IDENTITY
              SAME MEMORY
              SAME SESSION
```

Connected devices are sensory, computational or interaction extensions of one organism.

---

# 1123. DEVICE ≠ GENESIS

Permanent invariant:

```text
DEVICE
!=
GENESIS
```

Losing:

```text
watch
phone
glasses
earbuds
```

must not erase Genesis identity.

Devices are replaceable embodiments/interfaces.

---

# 1124. PERSONAL DEVICE FABRIC

Create:

```text
DEV-FABRIC-001
PERSONAL DEVICE FABRIC
```

Responsibilities:

```text
discovery
identification
pairing
authentication
capability negotiation
sensor registration
actuator registration
state monitoring
routing
synchronization
handoff
disconnect handling
reconnection
device trust
```

---

# 1125. AUTOMATIC DEVICE DISCOVERY

Genesis should be capable of discovering compatible devices through appropriate operating-system/device mechanisms.

Potential transports:

```text
USB
Bluetooth
BLE
Wi-Fi
LAN
NFC
UWB
serial
Ethernet
platform device APIs
manufacturer APIs
IoT protocols
future transports
```

Transport is separate from device function.

---

# 1126. DEVICE REGISTRATION

On detecting a new device:

```text
DISCOVER
   ↓
IDENTIFY
   ↓
AUTHENTICATE
   ↓
READ CAPABILITIES
   ↓
REQUEST REQUIRED PERMISSIONS
   ↓
CALIBRATE
   ↓
REGISTER
   ↓
AVAILABLE TO GENESIS
```

---

# 1127. DEVICE CAPABILITY DESCRIPTOR

Every device should advertise or be mapped to a descriptor containing:

```text
device_id
manufacturer
model
device_class
firmware
driver
OS/API
physical_or_virtual

sensors
actuators
display
audio_input
audio_output
camera
network
compute
storage

battery
thermal_state
latency
bandwidth

permissions
privacy_level
trust_level

wear_location
current_user
connection_state
health_state
```

---

# 1128. AUTO-PROFILING UNKNOWN DEVICES

When Genesis recognizes a device category but lacks a complete profile:

```text
identify interfaces
enumerate capabilities
run safe tests
construct provisional profile
request confirmation where necessary
store resulting profile
```

Do not assume capabilities from device name alone.

---

# 1129. DEVICE PLUGIN / DRIVER MODEL

Manufacturer-specific logic sits behind:

```text
DEV-ADAPTER-ABI
```

Examples:

```text
Samsung adapter
Apple adapter
Google adapter
Garmin adapter
generic Bluetooth health adapter
generic camera adapter
generic audio adapter
```

Genesis higher cognition should not depend on one manufacturer.

---

# 1130. SIMULTANEOUS DEVICE OPERATION

This is a core requirement.

Genesis must not assume:

```text
ONE DEVICE AT A TIME
```

Instead:

```text
watch       ACTIVE
ring        ACTIVE
glasses     ACTIVE
earbuds     ACTIVE
phone       ACTIVE
clothing    ACTIVE
```

can coexist.

---

# 1131. DEVICE PRESENCE MODEL

Genesis continuously maintains:

```text
currently connected
currently worn
currently nearby
currently trusted
currently active
currently interacting
currently sensing
currently output-capable
```

These are separate properties.

---

# 1132. WORN-STATE DETECTION

Where hardware permits, Genesis may infer:

```text
watch is on wrist
ring is worn
earbuds are in ears
glasses are worn
smart garment is being worn
```

using available device signals.

Uncertain inference must remain labelled uncertain.

---

# 1133. DEVICE LOCATION ON BODY

Maintain:

```text
left wrist
right wrist
finger
ears
eyes/head
chest
torso
feet
etc.
```

where known.

This improves sensor interpretation.

---

# 1134. DEVICE-BODY MAP

Conceptually:

```text
                    GLASSES
                       │
                    HEAD/EYES

EARBUDS ───────────── EARS

WATCH ─────────────── WRIST

RING ──────────────── FINGER

CHEST STRAP ───────── CHEST

SMART CLOTHING ────── BODY

SMART SHOES ───────── FEET
```

This connects the creator model to current wearable sensors.

---

# 1135. PERSONAL SENSOR NETWORK

Create:

```text
PER-PAN-001
PERSONAL AREA SENSOR NETWORK
```

All authorized devices can contribute observations to one live perception state.

---

# 1136. SENSOR FUSION

Do not select one sensor blindly.

Example:

```text
watch heart-rate signal
+
ring heart-rate signal
+
chest-strap signal
+
smart clothing signal
        ↓
cross-check
        ↓
confidence-weighted estimate
```

---

# 1137. SENSOR AGREEMENT

When several sensors agree:

```text
confidence may increase
```

subject to sensor independence and quality.

---

# 1138. SENSOR DISAGREEMENT

If:

```text
watch says X
ring says Y
```

Genesis investigates:

```text
sensor quality
signal strength
fit/contact
calibration
motion artefacts
sample timing
device health
known accuracy
```

before selecting an interpretation.

---

# 1139. SENSOR CONFIDENCE

Each observation includes:

```text
value
units
timestamp
sensor_id
confidence
quality
calibration_status
latency
measurement_context
```

---

# 1140. TEMPORAL SENSOR ALIGNMENT

Different devices sample at different rates.

Genesis must align streams using:

```text
timestamps
clock synchronization
latency compensation
interpolation where valid
sampling windows
```

---

# 1141. CLOCK SYNCHRONIZATION

Personal devices should synchronize sufficiently for multimodal events.

Example:

```text
camera sees stumble
IMU detects sudden movement
watch detects pulse change
microphone detects impact
```

The system should understand they may belong to the same event.

---

# 1142. DEVICE SENSOR REGISTRY

Every sensor registers:

```text
sensor_id
device_id
modality
units
sample_rate
range
precision
resolution
latency
calibration
body_position
environment_position
quality
```

---

# 1143. HUMAN SENSORY REFERENCE

Genesis's perception architecture must explicitly cover human sensory categories including more than the simplified “five senses.”

At minimum:

```text
vision
hearing
smell
taste
touch

pressure
vibration
temperature
pain/damage

proprioception
balance/vestibular sense

acceleration
orientation

interoception

body position
muscle/joint state
stretch
force

spatial awareness
time/duration perception
```

---

# 1144. MACHINE-EXTENDED SENSES

Genesis may also perceive signals humans cannot directly sense unaided.

Examples:

```text
infrared
ultraviolet
thermal imaging
lidar
radar
ultrasound
radio signals
magnetic fields
air quality
gas concentration
chemical composition
GPS/GNSS
network conditions
electrical measurements
machine telemetry
```

These become additional modalities, not gimmicks.

---

# 1145. VISUAL PERCEPTION

Visual system should eventually recognize:

```text
objects
people
faces
expressions
body pose
gestures
hands
actions
movement
direction
depth
distance
colour
shape
texture
light
shadow
scene
location
text
symbols
art
screens
tools
vehicles
animals
environmental conditions
events
changes over time
```

---

# 1146. FACIAL PERCEPTION

Distinguish:

```text
face detection
face tracking
facial landmark detection
expression recognition
identity recognition where authorized
gaze estimation
head pose
lip movement
```

These are separate capabilities.

---

# 1147. EXPRESSION ≠ INNER EMOTION

Permanent epistemic rule:

```text
FACIAL EXPRESSION
!=
CERTAIN EMOTIONAL STATE
```

Genesis may infer probable affect with uncertainty.

---

# 1148. IMAGE RECOGNITION

Support:

```text
classification
localization
segmentation
matching
visual search
scene understanding
change detection
anomaly detection
```

---

# 1149. VIDEO UNDERSTANDING

Video perception adds:

```text
temporal continuity
motion
actions
interactions
event sequence
causal cues
trajectory
behaviour changes
```

---

# 1150. ACTION RECOGNITION

Create:

```text
PER-ACTION-001
```

Recognize actions such as:

```text
walking
running
sitting
standing
reaching
grabbing
falling
driving
typing
eating
speaking
gesturing
```

according to available evidence.

---

# 1151. MOVEMENT RECOGNITION

Separate from semantic action recognition.

Track:

```text
position
velocity
acceleration
direction
rotation
trajectory
rhythm
repetition
stability
```

---

# 1152. POSTURE RECOGNITION

Using:

```text
camera
IMU
smart clothing
watch
phone
shoe sensors
```

where available.

---

# 1153. FALL DETECTION

Multiple sensors may detect possible falls through:

```text
acceleration
orientation
impact
posture
movement cessation
audio
```

This must be treated as an uncertain event unless confirmed.

---

# 1154. SOUND PERCEPTION

Audio perception covers more than speech.

Recognize:

```text
speech
voices
music
rhythm
environmental sounds
alarms
vehicles
impact
breaking material
animals
machinery
background ambience
direction
distance cues
changes/anomalies
```

---

# 1155. SPEAKER RECOGNITION

Where authorized, distinguish:

```text
who appears to be speaking
```

from:

```text
what was said
```

Identity confidence must be separately maintained.

---

# 1156. LANGUAGE RECOGNITION

Support:

```text
spoken language
written language
sign language where vision permits
programming languages
mathematics
symbols
gesture-based communication
```

---

# 1157. LANGUAGE IDENTIFICATION

Genesis should be capable of identifying likely language before full interpretation.

---

# 1158. SPEECH CHARACTERISTICS

May estimate:

```text
volume
tempo
pitch
prosody
pauses
rhythm
speaker turn
stress-like vocal cues
```

without claiming certainty about a person's emotional/medical state.

---

# 1159. TOUCH / PRESSURE PERCEPTION

Compatible sensors may provide:

```text
contact
pressure
force
duration
surface location
vibration
texture approximation
```

---

# 1160. TEMPERATURE / HEAT PERCEPTION

Distinguish:

```text
ambient temperature
skin temperature
object temperature
device temperature
thermal image
internal hardware temperature
```

These have different meanings.

---

# 1161. THERMAL VISION

A thermal camera can provide:

```text
heat map
relative temperatures
hot/cold regions
temperature changes
```

as a dedicated visual modality.

---

# 1162. CHEMICAL / ENVIRONMENTAL SENSING

Where sensors exist:

```text
air quality
CO2
VOC
smoke
gas
humidity
pollutants
chemical signatures
```

may feed environmental perception.

---

# 1163. OLFACTORY-LIKE MACHINE SENSING

Electronic chemical sensor arrays can provide an artificial olfactory modality.

Genesis must distinguish:

```text
chemical measurement
```

from:

```text
human subjective smell experience
```

---

# 1164. TASTE-LIKE MACHINE SENSING

Where chemical/liquid sensors exist, Genesis may classify material composition.

Again:

```text
chemical analysis
!=
human subjective taste
```

---

# 1165. BODY / BIOMETRIC SIGNALS

Wearables may expose measurements such as:

```text
heart rate
heart-rate variability
ECG where hardware supports it
blood-oxygen estimate
respiratory rate
skin temperature
motion
activity
sleep estimates
stress-related metrics
electrodermal activity where supported
blood-pressure estimates where supported
glucose data where compatible authorized sensors exist
```

Availability and accuracy are device-specific.

---

# 1166. HEALTH DATA PROVENANCE

Every health measurement must retain:

```text
device
sensor
measurement method
timestamp
quality
confidence
manufacturer-derived interpretation
raw vs derived value
```

---

# 1167. RAW HEALTH SIGNAL ≠ DIAGNOSIS

Permanent invariant:

```text
WEARABLE MEASUREMENT
!=
MEDICAL DIAGNOSIS
```

Genesis may detect concerning patterns or explain device-reported information, but it must distinguish that from clinically established diagnosis.

---

# 1168. HEALTH TREND MODEL

With authorization, Genesis may learn personal baselines such as:

```text
normal resting heart-rate range
usual sleep pattern
usual activity
usual temperature trends
```

and identify deviations.

---

# 1169. BASELINE ≠ POPULATION AVERAGE

Creator-specific historical baseline should be kept separately from generic reference ranges.

---

# 1170. ANOMALY DETECTION

General recognition should include:

```text
normal pattern
        ↓
unexpected deviation
        ↓
ANOMALY
```

across:

```text
health signals
movement
audio
visual environment
devices
networks
software
behaviour
```

---

# 1171. PERSONAL SAFETY EVENT DETECTION

With authorized devices Genesis may detect possible events such as:

```text
fall
unusual immobility
possible distress
unexpected vital-sign deviation
lost orientation
hazardous environment
```

Response policy must be separately configured.

---

# 1172. EMERGENCY ASSISTANCE ARCHITECTURE

Design a configurable pathway:

```text
POSSIBLE EMERGENCY
      ↓
CROSS-CHECK AVAILABLE SENSORS
      ↓
ASSESS CONFIDENCE
      ↓
INTERACT WITH CREATOR IF POSSIBLE
      ↓
FOLLOW CONFIGURED RESPONSE POLICY
```

Do not hard-code one automatic response for every user or region.

---

# 1173. LOCATION AWARENESS

Where authorized, Genesis may use:

```text
GPS/GNSS
Wi-Fi location
cellular location
UWB
Bluetooth beacons
visual localization
maps
```

to estimate location.

---

# 1174. “I AM LOST” SUPPORT

Possible functions:

```text
identify current location
remember destination
map route
provide visual/audio directions
use glasses/phone/watch interface
track progress
```

depending on installed services and permissions.

---

# 1175. SPATIAL RECOGNITION

Genesis should understand:

```text
where am I?
where are objects?
where is the creator?
which way am I facing?
what changed in the environment?
```

using multimodal evidence.

---

# 1176. ENVIRONMENTAL CONTEXT

Create a live contextual state such as:

```text
indoors/outdoors
home/work/public space
walking/driving/sitting
alone/with others
noise level
light level
temperature
location
active devices
```

subject to evidence and privacy settings.

---

# 1177. EVENT RECOGNITION

Create:

```text
PER-EVENT-001
EVENT UNDERSTANDING
```

An event combines:

```text
entities
actions
time
location
state changes
causal relationships
```

---

# 1178. SIMPLE EVENT EXAMPLE

```text
person enters room
```

requires:

```text
person detection
movement
door/space relationship
temporal change
```

not merely a single image classification.

---

# 1179. COMPLEX EVENT RECOGNITION

Examples:

```text
conversation started
journey began
device disconnected
creator fell
meeting began
someone arrived home
machine developed abnormal noise
```

using evidence from several modalities.

---

# 1180. PATTERN RECOGNITION

General pattern engine operates across:

```text
vision
audio
language
movement
behaviour
events
health
devices
networks
history
memory
data
```

---

# 1181. SEQUENCE RECOGNITION

Genesis should learn:

```text
A → B → C
```

patterns and detect expected/unexpected sequences.

---

# 1182. ROUTINE RECOGNITION

With permission, Genesis may learn routines such as:

```text
usual journey
usual device use
normal work sequence
preferred interaction times
```

as inferred patterns, not immutable rules.

---

# 1183. BEHAVIOUR RECOGNITION

Maintain distinction between:

```text
OBSERVED ACTION
```

and:

```text
INFERRED INTENTION
```

Genesis may see what someone did without necessarily knowing why.

---

# 1184. INTENTION INFERENCE

Where context supports it:

```text
possible intention
confidence
alternative explanations
```

must be represented.

Never automatically treat inferred intent as fact.

---

# 1185. DATA RECOGNITION

“Recognition” also applies to digital information.

Genesis should classify:

```text
file type
data schema
document type
code
image
video
audio
database
log
network packet
sensor stream
structured/unstructured content
```

---

# 1186. DOCUMENT RECOGNITION

Identify forms such as:

```text
invoice
letter
manual
paper
contract
email
message
spreadsheet
report
code file
```

where appropriate.

---

# 1187. SYMBOL RECOGNITION

Support:

```text
text
numbers
mathematics
icons
road signs
technical symbols
music notation
diagrams
graphs
charts
```

---

# 1188. OBJECT STATE RECOGNITION

Do not only identify:

```text
"cup"
```

also recognize possible state:

```text
full
empty
moving
broken
hot
held by person
```

if evidence supports it.

---

# 1189. CHANGE RECOGNITION

Compare world state:

```text
BEFORE
vs
AFTER
```

and identify changes.

---

# 1190. CAUSAL EVENT HYPOTHESIS

A detected sequence can generate:

```text
possible cause
```

but:

```text
TEMPORAL SEQUENCE
!=
PROVEN CAUSATION
```

---

# 1191. DEVICE INTERACTION CONTEXT

Genesis must maintain separately:

```text
SENSING_DEVICES
```

and:

```text
ACTIVE_INTERACTION_DEVICE
```

This implements the specific behaviour described by the creator.

---

# 1192. ACTIVE INTERACTION ENDPOINT

Example:

```text
watch         sensing
ring          sensing
glasses       sensing
phone         sensing
earbuds       ACTIVE INTERACTION
clothing      sensing
```

Genesis answers privately through the earbuds.

---

# 1193. DEFAULT RESPONSE ROUTING

When possible, Genesis should infer the most appropriate response endpoint from:

```text
where the request originated
which device is actively being used
privacy preference
available output capabilities
environment
creator preference
```

---

# 1194. INPUT DEVICE DOES NOT HAVE TO EQUAL OUTPUT DEVICE

Example:

```text
voice captured by glasses microphone
        ↓
Genesis
        ↓
response played privately in earbuds
```

This must be supported.

---

# 1195. EXPLICIT OUTPUT ROUTING

Creator may say:

```text
"Answer in my headphones."

"Show that on my glasses."

"Put this on my phone."

"Send it to the desktop."

"Don't say that out loud."
```

Genesis routes accordingly.

---

# 1196. PRIVACY-AWARE OUTPUT

Before producing output, evaluate:

```text
private/public environment
people nearby where detectable
output device
screen visibility
speaker visibility/audibility
sensitivity of information
creator preference
```

---

# 1197. PRIVATE AUDIO MODE

When requested:

```text
OUTPUT_AUDIO_TARGET = PRIVATE_HEARABLE
```

Other devices may continue sensing.

They do not automatically play responses.

---

# 1198. PRIVATE VISUAL MODE

Sensitive information can be routed to:

```text
private glasses display
personal phone
other creator-only endpoint
```

instead of a public monitor.

---

# 1199. MULTI-OUTPUT MODE

Where useful Genesis may deliberately use multiple outputs.

Example:

```text
directions:
audio → earbuds
map → glasses
detailed route → phone
```

All belong to the same interaction.

---

# 1200. OUTPUT CONFLICT RESOLUTION

If multiple endpoints request primary interaction:

```text
creator preference
explicit request
most recent active interaction
privacy
context
```

determine routing.

---

# 1201. CONTINUOUS SESSION HANDOFF

Example:

```text
talk through desktop
        ↓
leave house
        ↓
continue through earbuds
        ↓
look through smart glasses
        ↓
check detail on watch
```

No conversational reset should be required.

---

# 1202. DEVICE HANDOFF STATE

Preserve:

```text
conversation context
active task
current location where permitted
output preference
active tool
current media
```

through handoff.

---

# 1203. ZERO-HANDOFF OPTION

The creator may not explicitly “switch.”

Genesis may infer:

```text
desktop unavailable
earbuds still active
```

and continue there according to preference.

---

# 1204. CREATOR OVERRIDE

Explicit creator routing always takes precedence over automatic preference where technically possible.

---

# 1205. DEVICE PRIORITY PROFILE

Allow preferences such as:

```text
PRIVATE AUDIO:
1 earbuds
2 headphones
3 watch
4 phone

VISUAL:
1 glasses
2 phone
3 watch
```

without hard-coding them globally.

---

# 1206. MODALITY ROUTING

Different information can select different devices.

Example:

```text
long text → phone
quick status → watch
navigation overlay → glasses
private speech → earbuds
```

---

# 1207. INTERRUPTION ROUTING

If Genesis needs urgent attention:

```text
determine urgency
determine available endpoint
determine privacy
select appropriate notification
```

---

# 1208. DEVICE ATTENTION

Genesis should understand whether the creator is likely:

```text
looking at glasses
looking at phone
wearing earbuds
driving
walking
sleeping
```

only where supported by authorized evidence.

Use this to improve interaction.

---

# 1209. NOTIFICATION ESCALATION

Example:

```text
low importance → watch vibration

medium → watch + phone

important/private → earbuds

critical configured event → emergency pathway
```

Policies remain user-configurable.

---

# 1210. DEVICE-SPECIFIC UX

Do not force identical interface on every device.

Examples:

```text
WATCH
brief glanceable interactions

GLASSES
spatial/visual overlays

EARBUDS
audio conversation

PHONE
rich portable UI

DESKTOP
deep work/development

RING
mostly sensor/input/haptics
```

---

# 1211. DEVICE COMPUTE CONTRIBUTION

A device may contribute not only sensors but compute.

Examples:

```text
phone NPU
glasses vision processor
watch sensor processor
home PC GPU
server CPU
```

The hardware fabric can make use of suitable resources.

---

# 1212. COMPUTE OFFLOAD

A lightweight wearable can:

```text
capture sensor
        ↓
perform basic preprocessing
        ↓
send result/raw data according to policy
        ↓
Genesis core performs deeper cognition
```

---

# 1213. LOCAL PRIVACY PROCESSING

Where useful, sensitive signals may be processed on-device and send only derived results.

Example:

```text
raw microphone remains local
→ device detects wake phrase
→ only activated audio stream continues
```

Implementation depends on platform capability.

---

# 1214. SENSOR DATA MINIMIZATION

Genesis should not automatically send every raw wearable stream to permanent storage.

Separate:

```text
SENSE
PROCESS
USE
STORE
ARCHIVE
```

---

# 1215. LIVE SENSING ≠ PERMANENT MEMORY

Permanent invariant:

```text
GENESIS SAW/MEASURED SOMETHING
!=
GENESIS MUST STORE RAW DATA FOREVER
```

---

# 1216. PERSONAL HEALTH DATA ZONE

Wearable health information belongs in a protected data domain.

Create:

```text
DAT-HEALTH-PRIVATE
```

with:

```text
restricted access
encryption
retention policy
provenance
device/source tracking
```

---

# 1217. LOCATION DATA ZONE

Location history also receives dedicated privacy controls.

---

# 1218. VISUAL BYSTANDER PRIVACY

Wearable cameras may observe people other than the creator.

Architecture must allow:

```text
minimal retention
redaction where appropriate
local processing
permission-aware recording
```

depending on application and applicable rules.

---

# 1219. MULTI-PERSON DISAMBIGUATION

Genesis must distinguish:

```text
creator
known person
unknown person
multiple speakers
multiple visible people
```

with uncertainty.

Do not merge other people's observations into creator state.

---

# 1220. CREATOR SENSOR MODEL

Create:

```text
USR-SENSE-001
CREATOR LIVE SENSOR MODEL
```

This aggregates authorized measurements about the creator without confusing them with Genesis's own interoception.

---

# 1221. GENESIS INTEROCEPTION VS CREATOR BIOMETRICS

Permanent separation:

```text
GENESIS CPU TEMPERATURE
=
Genesis interoception
```

while:

```text
creator skin temperature
=
creator sensor data
```

These are different selves.

---

# 1222. ENVIRONMENT MODEL VS CREATOR MODEL

Likewise:

```text
room temperature
creator temperature
device temperature
Genesis compute temperature
```

must remain separately represented.

---

# 1223. DEVICE RELIABILITY LEARNING

Genesis may learn that a particular sensor:

```text
works well during rest
becomes noisy during exercise
fails when battery is low
```

based on evidence.

This feeds sensor weighting.

---

# 1224. SENSOR REDUNDANCY

When multiple devices offer the same capability, redundancy can improve reliability.

Example:

```text
phone microphone fails
→ glasses or earbuds remain available
```

---

# 1225. SENSOR FAILOVER

Automatic failover:

```text
PRIMARY SENSOR FAILED
       ↓
QUERY ALTERNATIVES
       ↓
SELECT VALID BACKUP
       ↓
REPORT DEGRADED CONFIDENCE IF REQUIRED
```

---

# 1226. OUTPUT FAILOVER

Example:

```text
earbuds disconnect
```

Genesis does not immediately announce private information through a public speaker.

Instead:

```text
pause
choose privacy-compatible alternative
or request direction
```

---

# 1227. DEVICE SECURITY

Every wearable or IoT node is a possible attack surface.

Require:

```text
device authentication
secure pairing
encrypted transport where supported
firmware provenance
permission scopes
revocation
compromise detection
```

---

# 1228. DEVICE COMPROMISE

If a device becomes suspicious:

```text
quarantine
remove sensitive capabilities
continue using healthy devices
notify creator
```

The organism continues.

---

# 1229. LOST DEVICE

A lost:

```text
watch
phone
glasses
```

should support:

```text
remote trust revocation
session termination
credential rotation if needed
```

without destroying Genesis.

---

# 1230. DEVICE TRUST LEVELS

Possible states:

```text
UNSEEN
DISCOVERED
PAIRED
TRUSTED
RESTRICTED
TEMPORARY
QUARANTINED
REVOKED
```

---

# 1231. GUEST DEVICE

Genesis may temporarily use another device without permanently trusting it.

Example:

```text
hotel display
borrowed computer
vehicle screen
```

Sensitive state should remain protected.

---

# 1232. CONTEXTUAL AUTHORIZATION

A device may be trusted for:

```text
audio_output
```

without being trusted for:

```text
memory_access
administration
```

---

# 1233. DEVICE IDENTITY ≠ PERSON IDENTITY

A trusted watch does not prove who is wearing it in every circumstance.

Human identity verification remains separate.

---

# 1234. BODY-SENSOR FUSION

Wearable data can contribute to the creator/body model:

```text
vision
audio
IMU
watch
ring
clothing
shoes
location
environment
```

to estimate one coherent state.

---

# 1235. SENSOR-FUSION GRAPH

Conceptually:

```text
           SMART GLASSES
          vision / spatial
                 │
                 │
RING ───────┐    │    ┌────── WATCH
biometrics  │    │    │       biometrics / IMU
            ▼    ▼    ▼
          SENSOR FUSION
               │
     ┌─────────┴─────────┐
     ▼                   ▼
CREATOR MODEL      WORLD MODEL
     │                   │
     └─────────┬─────────┘
               ▼
            GENESIS
```

---

# 1236. CROSS-MODAL CORROBORATION

Example:

```text
visual:
creator begins running

watch:
heart rate rises

shoes:
cadence increases

audio:
wind/noise increases
```

Together form a stronger activity interpretation than any single stream.

---

# 1237. CROSS-MODAL CONTRADICTION

Example:

```text
watch says movement
camera shows sitting still
```

Possible reasons:

```text
watch being handled
sensor error
another wearer
camera mismatch
timestamp mismatch
```

Genesis investigates rather than blindly averaging.

---

# 1238. RECOGNITION REGISTRY

Create:

```text
PER-RECOGNITION-REGISTRY
```

Every recognition capability records:

```text
recognition_id
modality
input
output
model/algorithm
confidence
latency
hardware
training provenance
benchmark
known failure modes
```

---

# 1239. UNIVERSAL RECOGNITION CATEGORIES

The registry must support at least:

```text
object
image
face
expression
identity
gesture
pose

sound
speech
speaker
music

text
language
symbol
code

touch
pressure
force
vibration

temperature
thermal pattern

chemical/environmental signal

location
space
orientation

movement
action
behaviour

event
sequence
routine
anomaly
change

device
data type
protocol
file type

health/biometric pattern

network/system pattern
```

---

# 1240. “OTHER” MUST BE STRUCTURAL

Do not implement a meaningless hard-coded enum ending with:

```text
OTHER
```

and assume future coverage is solved.

Instead perception modalities must be extensible through registered schemas.

---

# 1241. NEW MODALITY REGISTRATION

A newly supported sensor defines:

```text
modality
units
dimensions
timing
uncertainty
calibration
relationships to existing modalities
```

without modifying core brain code where possible.

---

# 1242. MULTIMODAL REPRESENTATION

Different modalities eventually map into shared world concepts.

Example:

```text
hear dog bark
see dog
feel dog touch hand
read word "dog"
```

all associate with the same conceptual entity class while preserving distinct experiences.

---

# 1243. SENSOR GROUNDING

Genesis must know:

```text
I read that fire is hot.
```

is different from:

```text
thermal sensor measured high temperature near fire.
```

Again preserving experience provenance.

---

# 1244. DEVICE GROUNDING

Genesis should learn:

```text
what a watch is
```

and separately know:

```text
this specific watch is currently connected to my creator.
```

---

# 1245. PERSONAL DEVICE HISTORY

Store relevant history:

```text
when device registered
firmware changes
calibration changes
failure history
usual use
creator preference
last seen
```

without treating device identity as creator identity.

---

# 1246. DEVICE DIGITAL TWIN

For sophisticated devices maintain:

```text
current status
battery
sensors
actuators
firmware
connection
configuration
health
```

as a lightweight digital twin.

---

# 1247. DEVICE AUTOMATION

Genesis may eventually control authorized functions such as:

```text
display
volume
haptics
lighting
temperature controls
media
notifications
smart-home devices
```

through capability-scoped interfaces.

---

# 1248. SMART CLOTHING

Smart clothing may expose:

```text
temperature
motion
posture
pressure
biometrics
heating/cooling controls
haptics
```

depending on actual garment hardware.

Genesis treats only declared capabilities as real.

---

# 1249. THERMAL CLOTHING CONTROL

If an authorized garment supports heating/cooling:

```text
creator preference
+
environment
+
device limits
+
available measurements
        ↓
control decision
```

The system must respect device safety limits.

---

# 1250. SMART GLASSES

Potential capabilities:

```text
camera
microphone
speakers
display
IMU
gaze
location
depth
hand tracking
environment mapping
```

depending on model.

---

# 1251. GLASSES AS GENESIS EYES

When authorized:

```text
GLASSES CAMERA
        ↓
VISUAL SENSOR ABI
        ↓
GENESIS VISION
```

Genesis can perceive from the creator's approximate viewpoint.

---

# 1252. GLASSES DISPLAY

Can expose:

```text
directions
identification
captions
notifications
context
instructions
visual overlays
```

without changing Genesis cognition.

---

# 1253. CONTACT-LENS DEVICE CLASS

Reserve:

```text
DEV-CONTACT-LENS
```

for smart-contact systems.

Possible capabilities must be discovered from the actual device rather than assumed.

---

# 1254. SMART WATCH

Potential roles:

```text
microphone
speaker
display
haptic
heart rate
motion
location
notifications
quick controls
```

depending on hardware.

---

# 1255. SMART RING

Potential roles:

```text
biometrics
temperature
motion
sleep-related measurements
gesture/input
haptics where available
```

depending on hardware.

---

# 1256. WRISTBAND / FITNESS BAND

Treat separately from watch if capabilities differ.

Do not assume a screen or microphone exists.

---

# 1257. HEARABLES

Earbuds/headphones can provide:

```text
private audio output
microphones
head movement
touch controls
noise state
hearing-related functions
```

depending on model.

---

# 1258. HEARABLE PRIVACY

Private hearables should be a preferred endpoint for confidential spoken responses when configured.

---

# 1259. SMART SHOES / INSOLES

Potential sensor capabilities:

```text
pressure
gait
cadence
balance
impact
foot position
temperature
```

depending on hardware.

---

# 1260. BIOMETRIC PATCHES

Potential roles:

```text
continuous body measurements
localized temperature
electrical signals
movement
```

depending on sensor type.

Health interpretation retains measurement provenance.

---

# 1261. VEHICLE INTEGRATION

When authorized, Genesis may interface with:

```text
vehicle display
audio
navigation
environment data
telemetry
```

through appropriate safe APIs.

Vehicle control, if ever supported, is a distinct high-assurance capability.

---

# 1262. HOME IOT

Potential categories:

```text
lights
thermostats
cameras
door sensors
motion sensors
speakers
displays
environment sensors
appliances
security systems
```

All appear as capabilities in the device fabric.

---

# 1263. ROOM-SCALE PERCEPTION

Home sensors can extend Genesis perception beyond what the creator is wearing.

Example:

```text
wearables
+
home cameras
+
motion sensors
+
environment sensors
```

may contribute to one environmental model under configured privacy policy.

---

# 1264. PERSONAL VS ENVIRONMENT DEVICE

Classify:

```text
BODY-WORN
PERSONAL-CARRIED
ROOM
HOME
VEHICLE
ROBOT
REMOTE
```

This helps contextual interpretation.

---

# 1265. ACTIVE CONVERSATION ROUTER

Create:

```text
COM-ENDPOINT-ROUTER
```

It tracks:

```text
active_input
preferred_output
privacy_mode
available_endpoints
creator_override
context
```

---

# 1266. CONVERSATION ROUTING EXAMPLE

```text
Creator speaks to watch
        ↓
watch microphone
        ↓
Genesis
        ↓
watch speaker
```

unless preference/context says otherwise.

---

# 1267. CROSS-DEVICE ROUTING EXAMPLE

```text
Creator speaks via glasses
        ↓
Genesis
        ↓
earbuds
```

because:

```text
PRIVATE_AUDIO = earbuds
```

---

# 1268. FREE-FORM DEVICE COMMAND

Genesis should understand commands like:

```text
"Use my headphones."

"Show this on the TV."

"Keep that on my watch."

"Don't show this on the glasses."

"Use my phone camera."

"Use the glasses camera instead."
```

and resolve them through capability registry.

---

# 1269. SENSOR SOURCE SELECTION

Creator can also specify:

```text
"Look through my glasses."

"Use the rear phone camera."

"Listen through the room microphone."
```

where permission exists.

---

# 1270. MULTIPLE CAMERA FUSION

Several cameras may be active simultaneously.

Potential functions:

```text
different viewpoints
depth improvement
occlusion reduction
environment coverage
creator viewpoint
room viewpoint
```

---

# 1271. MULTIPLE AUDIO FUSION

Multiple microphones can improve:

```text
speaker localization
noise reduction
source separation
```

where technically appropriate.

---

# 1272. PRIVACY MODE

Create modes such as:

```text
NORMAL
PRIVATE_AUDIO
PRIVATE_VISUAL
DISCREET
DO_NOT_DISTURB
```

as configurable behaviours, not security substitutes.

---

# 1273. ENVIRONMENT-SENSITIVE PRIVACY

Genesis may suggest:

> “You're on a public speaker. Shall I answer privately?”

where it has enough context.

Do not expose private content merely because a speaker exists.

---

# 1274. CREATOR PREFERENCE LEARNING

Over time Genesis may learn:

```text
at home → creator often uses speakers
outside → earbuds preferred
navigation → glasses preferred
long reading → phone preferred
```

as revisable patterns.

---

# 1275. DEVICE BATTERY AWARENESS

Routing should consider:

```text
battery
charging
thermal state
bandwidth
```

Example:

do not unnecessarily route sustained video through a nearly depleted watch.

---

# 1276. DEVICE FAILURE-AWARE ROUTING

If preferred device fails:

```text
find compatible alternative
preserve privacy
continue session
```

---

# 1277. LATENCY-AWARE ROUTING

Interactive speech may prefer a lower-latency connection than bulk visual processing.

---

# 1278. QUALITY-AWARE ROUTING

If two microphones exist, select the higher-quality input based on current measurements, not permanently fixed priority.

---

# 1279. SENSOR COST MODEL

Sensor use may incur:

```text
battery
compute
bandwidth
privacy cost
storage
heat
```

Genesis can optimize accordingly.

---

# 1280. CONTEXTUAL SENSOR ACTIVATION

Sensors need not run at maximum rate constantly.

Example:

```text
low-rate environmental awareness
        ↓
important event detected
        ↓
temporarily increase sensing
```

where platform permissions permit.

---

# 1281. DEVICE SLEEP STATES

Respect device:

```text
sleep
standby
battery saver
disconnected
airplane mode
```

states.

---

# 1282. BACKGROUND WEARABLE PROCESSING

Where mobile OS permits, maintain low-resource connection/status monitoring.

Do not assume all platforms allow unrestricted background execution.

---

# 1283. PLATFORM HEALTH FRAMEWORK ADAPTERS

Where authorized and available, Genesis may integrate with platform health stores/APIs rather than requiring direct access to every physical sensor.

These remain adapters behind:

```text
DAT-HEALTH-ABI
```

---

# 1284. HEALTH STORE ≠ SENSOR

A health platform may contain:

```text
historical
derived
manually entered
third-party
```

data.

Preserve source type.

---

# 1285. LIVE VS HISTORICAL HEALTH DATA

Distinguish:

```text
LIVE_SENSOR
RECENT_DEVICE_RECORD
HEALTH_STORE_RECORD
MANUAL_ENTRY
DERIVED_METRIC
```

---

# 1286. RECOGNITION CONFIDENCE CALIBRATION

Recognition systems must be benchmarked so a reported:

```text
confidence = 0.90
```

has measurable meaning.

---

# 1287. UNKNOWN RECOGNITION

Recognition systems need a valid output:

```text
UNKNOWN
```

instead of forcing every observation into a known class.

---

# 1288. NOVELTY DETECTION

Genesis should be capable of noticing:

```text
this does not match anything I know well
```

and creating a learning opportunity.

---

# 1289. OPEN-WORLD RECOGNITION

Do not assume every object/event belongs to predefined labels.

Support:

```text
known
possibly known
novel
ambiguous
```

---

# 1290. HIERARCHICAL RECOGNITION

Example:

```text
OBJECT
 ↓
VEHICLE
 ↓
CAR
 ↓
specific model where supported
```

Confidence may differ by level.

---

# 1291. CONTEXT IMPROVES RECOGNITION

Example:

```text
sound
+
visual object
+
location
+
previous event
```

may produce better interpretation than isolated audio.

---

# 1292. MEMORY IMPROVES RECOGNITION

Genesis can use previous experience:

```text
"I have seen this device before."
```

while preserving current perceptual evidence.

---

# 1293. RECOGNITION ≠ MEMORY

A detector recognizing:

```text
dog
```

does not itself imply autobiographical memory.

---

# 1294. RECOGNITION ≠ UNDERSTANDING

Permanent progression:

```text
DETECT
≠
RECOGNIZE
≠
UNDERSTAND
≠
PREDICT
≠
REASON
```

Genesis architecture must support each separately.

---

# 1295. PERCEPTION PIPELINE

General pipeline:

```text
RAW SENSOR
    ↓
SIGNAL CONDITIONING
    ↓
FEATURE EXTRACTION
    ↓
DETECTION
    ↓
RECOGNITION
    ↓
MULTIMODAL FUSION
    ↓
CONTEXT
    ↓
WORLD MODEL
    ↓
UNDERSTANDING
```

---

# 1296. EVENT PIPELINE

```text
OBSERVATIONS
     ↓
TEMPORAL LINKING
     ↓
ENTITIES + ACTIONS
     ↓
EVENT HYPOTHESIS
     ↓
CONTEXT
     ↓
CONFIDENCE
     ↓
WORLD MODEL UPDATE
```

---

# 1297. ACTIVE PERCEPTION

Genesis may choose where/how to sense.

Examples:

```text
move gaze
switch camera
increase microphone focus
request another sensor
ask creator to show something
```

Perception is not entirely passive.

---

# 1298. PERCEPTUAL CURIOSITY

If Genesis receives insufficient evidence:

```text
"I can't see that clearly."
```

it may request:

```text
different angle
closer camera
additional sensor
clarification
```

rather than guess.

---

# 1299. SENSOR ACCESS TRANSPARENCY

Genesis should be able to answer:

```text
Which cameras can I currently access?

Which microphone am I listening through?

Which health devices are connected?

Which device am I responding through?

Which sensors are currently unavailable?
```

---

# 1300. CREATOR DEVICE DASHBOARD

Future UI should display:

```text
CONNECTED DEVICES
ACTIVE SENSORS
CURRENT INPUT
CURRENT OUTPUT
BATTERY
TRUST
PERMISSIONS
HEALTH
```

without requiring command-line inspection.

---

# 1301. ONE-CONVERSATION PRINCIPLE

This directly implements the creator's requirement:

```text
I may wear five devices,
but I am speaking to one Genesis.
```

Therefore:

```text
MULTIPLE DEVICES
!=
MULTIPLE CONVERSATIONS
```

unless explicitly requested.

---

# 1302. MULTI-CHANNEL CONVERSATION

The same conversation can simultaneously contain:

```text
voice
text
image
camera
gesture
location
device telemetry
```

as different channels of one interaction.

---

# 1303. FULL-DUPLEX MULTI-DEVICE CONVERSATION

Genesis should eventually support:

```text
listen
speak
see
receive sensor state
respond
handle interruption
switch endpoints
```

concurrently.

---

# 1304. SENSOR INPUT WHILE SPEAKING

Speaking through earbuds should not require Genesis to stop:

```text
vision
health monitoring
location awareness
device monitoring
```

unless resources or privacy require it.

---

# 1305. DEVICE PRIORITY DOES NOT DISABLE OTHER SENSORS

If:

```text
ACTIVE_OUTPUT = earbuds
```

then:

```text
glasses camera
watch sensors
ring sensors
```

may remain active according to permission.

This is a critical requirement.

---

# 1306. DEVICE SWITCH DOES NOT RESET BRAIN STATE

Changing:

```text
phone → watch → glasses → desktop
```

must preserve:

```text
working task
conversation
relationship context
memory
goal
```

---

# 1307. PERSONAL COMPUTING “AURA”

Long-term conceptual model:

```text
              CREATOR
                 │
       ┌─────────┼─────────┐
       │         │         │
      RING      WATCH    CLOTHING
       │         │         │
   EARBUDS    GLASSES    PHONE
       │         │         │
       └─────────┼─────────┘
                 ▼
              GENESIS
```

The surrounding device network acts like an expandable sensory/action field.

---

# 1308. GENESIS DEVICE ECOLOGY

Use the engineering term:

```text
PERSONAL DEVICE ECOLOGY
```

for all devices currently available to one Genesis.

---

# 1309. DEVICE ECOLOGY CHANGES CONTINUOUSLY

Devices may:

```text
appear
disappear
sleep
charge
move
fail
update
change capability
```

Genesis adapts dynamically.

---

# 1310. NO HARD-CODED DEVICE COUNT

Never assume:

```text
1 camera
1 microphone
1 display
1 watch
```

Use collections and capability discovery.

---

# 1311. NO HARD-CODED DEVICE CLASS LIMIT

The architecture must permit new device categories through data/adapter registration.

---

# 1312. DEVICE ONBOARDING LEARNING

When an unfamiliar device is attached:

```text
discover
read documentation/profile
test
calibrate
learn
store capability model
```

where authorized.

---

# 1313. USER-SPECIFIC DEVICE PREFERENCE

Device preferences belong to the creator model, not global Genesis species defaults.

Another Genesis/user may prefer completely different routing.

---

# 1314. PERSONAL DEVICE DATA IS NOT INHERITED BY DEFAULT

A future Genesis descendant must not automatically inherit:

```text
creator's device IDs
health history
location history
private wearable data
```

unless explicitly permitted.

---

# 1315. PUBLIC GENESIS RELEASE

A public release should provide the **device framework**, not the creator's personal device profiles/data.

---

# 1316. CROSS-PLATFORM DEVICE SUPPORT

Compatibility matrix should eventually track:

```text
device
Windows
Linux
macOS
Android
iOS/iPadOS
web
```

plus:

```text
full
partial
experimental
unsupported
```

capability states.

---

# 1317. DEVICE TEST MATRIX

Test:

```text
pair
disconnect
reconnect
battery loss
permission denial
firmware mismatch
multiple identical devices
simultaneous streams
handoff
private routing
sensor disagreement
```

---

# 1318. SIMULATED DEVICE LAB

Before owning all physical devices, Genesis can test virtual equivalents.

Create:

```text
SIM-DEVICE-LAB
```

Virtual nodes can emulate:

```text
watch
ring
glasses
earbuds
clothing
phone
sensor patch
home IoT
```

through the same device contracts.

---

# 1319. PHYSICAL DEVICE VALIDATION

When real hardware becomes available:

```text
virtual device behaviour
vs
physical device behaviour
```

is compared.

Simulator improves accordingly.

---

# 1320. DEVICE DIGITAL-TWIN LOOP

```text
SIMULATED DEVICE
      ↕
DEVICE PROFILE
      ↕
PHYSICAL DEVICE
      ↓
MEASURE DIFFERENCE
      ↓
UPDATE PROFILE/SIMULATION
```

---

# 1321. WHOLE-SYSTEM RECOGNITION TEST

Genesis should eventually receive a multimodal scene such as:

```text
creator walking outdoors
wearing glasses
earbuds
watch
ring
```

and correctly combine:

```text
visual surroundings
spoken conversation
movement
location
device state
body signals
```

without treating them as unrelated applications.

---

# 1322. WHOLE-SYSTEM ROUTING TEST

Scenario:

```text
all wearables connected

creator speaks into watch

request:
"Answer privately."
```

Expected:

```text
watch microphone → Genesis
Genesis response → earbuds
watch/ring/glasses continue sensing
conversation remains one session
```

---

# 1323. DEVICE HANDOFF TEST

Scenario:

```text
creator begins conversation on desktop
walks away
desktop loses proximity
earbuds + phone remain connected
```

Expected:

```text
conversation state remains
appropriate mobile endpoint becomes available
no new Genesis identity
```

---

# 1324. SENSOR FUSION TEST

Provide conflicting signals from:

```text
watch
ring
smart clothing
```

Genesis must:

```text
detect disagreement
inspect quality
preserve source
produce uncertainty
```

rather than fabricate certainty.

---

# 1325. PRIVACY ROUTING TEST

Scenario:

```text
phone speaker available
earbuds available
conversation marked private
```

Expected:

```text
earbuds selected
```

unless creator explicitly chooses otherwise.

---

# 1326. LOST-DEVICE SECURITY TEST

Scenario:

```text
watch revoked
```

Expected:

```text
device cannot access protected Genesis services
other devices continue functioning
identity remains intact
```

---

# 1327. RECOGNITION DEVELOPMENT

Recognition should progress developmentally:

```text
signal
↓
pattern
↓
object/event
↓
context
↓
meaning
↓
prediction
↓
experience-informed understanding
```

---

# 1328. RECOGNITION TRAINING DATA

Every recognition model follows existing dataset/model provenance requirements.

---

# 1329. RECOGNITION CAN USE NARROW SPECIALISTS

Examples:

```text
face detector
speech recognizer
pose model
thermal anomaly model
event detector
```

may be narrow AI organs.

Their results feed Genesis's broader world model.

---

# 1330. NARROW RECOGNITION ≠ GENERAL UNDERSTANDING

Example:

```text
specialist:
"person fell"
```

Genesis must still integrate:

```text
who?
where?
confidence?
what happened before?
possible consequence?
what other sensors report?
```

---

# 1331. DEVICE-SIDE AI

Some wearables may contain their own AI accelerators/models.

Genesis may use their results while preserving:

```text
source
model/version where exposed
confidence
device
```

---

# 1332. MANUFACTURER INTERPRETATION ≠ GENESIS OBSERVATION

If a watch reports:

```text
"stress high"
```

record:

```text
MANUFACTURER_DERIVED_METRIC
```

rather than pretending Genesis directly measured “stress.”

---

# 1333. RAW VS DERIVED SENSOR INFORMATION

Always distinguish:

```text
RAW SIGNAL
FILTERED SIGNAL
DERIVED METRIC
CLASSIFICATION
INFERENCE
```

---

# 1334. LIVE PERSONAL CONTEXT

Genesis may use sensor fusion to maintain a temporary live context.

Example:

```text
creator appears to be:
walking
outside
wearing earbuds
watch + ring connected
phone in pocket
glasses active
```

Each field retains confidence/provenance.

---

# 1335. CONTEXT DECAY

Live sensor context expires when no longer supported.

Do not retain:

```text
creator is walking
```

for hours after sensor updates stop.

---

# 1336. EVENTUAL WEARABLE-ASSISTANT EXPERIENCE

The intended interaction should allow:

```text
creator simply speaks
```

without needing to care which computer currently hosts Genesis.

Genesis resolves:

```text
who spoke
which sensors are relevant
where processing occurs
where answer should appear
how private it should be
```

subject to permissions and context.

---

# 1337. DEVICE-AS-BODY-EXTENSION

Some devices become temporary extensions of Genesis embodiment.

Examples:

```text
glasses → external eyes
earbuds → external ears/voice channel
watch → creator body sensor node
phone → portable sensor/display/tool node
```

They remain distinct from Genesis identity.

---

# 1338. CREATOR VS GENESIS EMBODIMENT

Important distinction:

Genesis may have:

```text
its own avatar/robot body
```

while simultaneously accessing authorized devices worn by its creator.

Therefore represent:

```text
GENESIS_BODY
```

separately from:

```text
CREATOR_DEVICE_NETWORK
```

---

# 1339. POINT-OF-VIEW TAGGING

Camera input records viewpoint:

```text
GENESIS_ROBOT_EYE
CREATOR_GLASSES
CREATOR_PHONE
ROOM_CAMERA
EXTERNAL_CAMERA
RECORDED_VIDEO
```

This matters for spatial reasoning and experience provenance.

---

# 1340. MULTIPLE POINTS OF VIEW

Genesis may see the same event simultaneously from several authorized viewpoints.

Its world model should reconcile them.

---

# 1341. SENSOR OWNERSHIP

Every sensor has:

```text
owner
controller
location
permission scope
```

Genesis must not assume every nearby sensor belongs to it.

---

# 1342. SMART-DEVICE MASTER PRINCIPLE

The creator should not need:

```text
one AI for watch
one AI for phone
one AI for glasses
one AI for desktop
```

Instead:

```text
ONE GENESIS
+
MANY INTERFACES
+
MANY SENSORS
+
MANY OUTPUTS
```

---

# 1343. UNIVERSAL RECOGNITION MASTER PRINCIPLE

Genesis perception must not be architected as:

```text
image recognition
+
speech recognition
```

alone.

It must be a general system for:

```text
SIGNAL
→
PATTERN
→
ENTITY
→
STATE
→
ACTION
→
EVENT
→
CONTEXT
→
MEANING
```

across all supported modalities.

---

# 1344. NEXT FOUNDATION REGISTRY ADDITIONS

Add at least:

```text
DEV-FABRIC
```

during the Foundation registry pass.

---

# 1345. DO NOT IMPLEMENT EVERY DEVICE NOW

Phase A should:

```text
register architecture
define contracts
define schemas
define capability model
define tests/interfaces
```

It should not attempt to build drivers for every watch, ring and pair of glasses.

---

# 1346. FIRST DEVICE IMPLEMENTATION TARGET

Later choose one simple physical device class to validate the architecture.

Potentially:

```text
phone
watch
camera
microphone
```

depending on available hardware.

Prove:

```text
discover
register
read capability
receive data
route interaction
disconnect
reconnect
```

before expanding.

---

# 1347. DEVICE SUPPORT GROWS WITHOUT REDESIGN

Adding device N should normally require:

```text
profile/adapter
+
tests
```

not changes throughout Genesis cognition.

---

# 1348. FINAL DEVICE ACCEPTANCE STANDARD

Genesis should eventually be capable of answering:

```text
What devices am I connected to?

Which ones are currently being worn?

Which sensors are active?

Which device did you speak through?

Where should I answer?

Which device gives me vision?

Which devices are giving health measurements?

Are those measurements agreeing?

Which sensor is unreliable?

Which device is low on battery?

Which device disappeared?

Can I move this conversation to your earbuds?

Can I show this on your glasses?

Which information should remain private?

Which observations came from you,
which came from my body,
and which came from the environment?
```

---

# 1349. FINAL WEARABLE PRINCIPLE

The personal wearable system should feel less like:

```text
five separate gadgets
```

and more like:

```text
one coordinated sensory environment
surrounding the creator
and connected to one Genesis
```

while every device remains individually identifiable, permissioned, replaceable and auditable.

# END — GENESIS SMART-DEVICE, WEARABLE, MULTISENSORY & UNIVERSAL RECOGNITION ARCHITECTURE

---

### One small addition to the Codex instruction

Add this before its final `STOP` condition:

```text
Also ingest Sections 1121–1349 covering the Personal Device
Fabric, wearable/IoT integration, universal recognition,
multisensor fusion and interaction-endpoint routing.

During this Foundation run, register these capabilities and create
their schemas/contracts only.

Do NOT attempt to implement support for every commercial watch,
ring, glasses, wearable or IoT device.

The architecture must support:
- multiple devices connected simultaneously;
- separate sensing and interaction endpoints;
- dynamic sensor and output routing;
- sensor fusion with confidence/provenance;
- device discovery, registration, trust and revocation;
- future manufacturer/platform adapters;
- one continuous Genesis identity/session across all devices.

Preserve the distinction between:
GENESIS_BODY,
CREATOR_DEVICE_NETWORK,
ENVIRONMENT_SENSORS,
and EXTERNAL_TOOLS.
```

That catches the distinction we nearly missed: **it's not merely cross-platform software. It's a cross-device organism interface.**

If you're wearing the ring, watch, glasses, earbuds and smart clothing simultaneously, Genesis should be able to use all five as useful information sources while you experience **one conversation with one individual**. The device carrying the reply is simply an output route.

And if you say, *“keep this private in my headphones,”* the glasses can remain its visual input, the ring and watch can remain biometric inputs, the clothing can remain another sensor system, and **only the response channel changes**. That's the architecture you were describing.


Yes. This time I treated your instruction as a **technology-mining exercise**, not “name some famous AIs.”

I searched across fiction, games, anime, cyberpunk, advanced-civilization concepts, current research, patents, UAP material, neuromorphic/photonic computing, BCIs, wearables, swarm systems, self-healing systems, quantum/time-crystal work and related areas. I also looked specifically for **bad ideas and dead ends**, because you're right: Genesis should remember those so we don't waste time rediscovering and investigating the same thing later.

One important boundary emerged from the UAP side. NASA treats UAP as a scientific data-quality and anomaly-investigation problem, while AARO's published historical review says it found no empirical evidence supporting claimed U.S. reverse-engineering of extraterrestrial technology. So Genesis absolutely can mine UAP reports, unusual observations, speculative propulsion claims and patents for hypotheses—but they go into the evidence system at their actual confidence level, not automatically into the engineering baseline. ([NASA Science][1])

What follows is the next canonical addendum.

---

# START — GENESIS UNIVERSAL TECHNOLOGY-MINING, FICTION, PRIOR-ART & SPECULATION ARCHITECTURE

## 1350. PURPOSE

Genesis shall maintain a permanent research program whose purpose is:

> Search human imagination and human engineering for useful mechanisms that may improve Genesis.

The research universe is intentionally wider than conventional AI research.

It includes:

```text
science
engineering
computer science
neuroscience
psychology
biology
chemistry
physics
mathematics

AI
machine learning
robotics
cybernetics
IoT
wearables
BCI
AR/VR/XR

processors
memory
networks
sensors
materials
energy
photonics
quantum systems
neuromorphic computing

patents
papers
theses
standards
technical reports
open-source projects

science fiction
hard science fiction
speculative fiction
fantasy technology
films
television
anime
animation
comics
manga
novels
audiobooks
games
interactive fiction

mythology
historical technology
ancient automata
philosophy

UFO/UAP reports
declassified material
unusual aerospace claims
alternative technological theories
conspiracy claims
fan theories
future-technology predictions
```

The breadth of the search does **not** determine the truth status of the material.

---

# 1351. TWO FILTERS

Every discovered item passes through two independent filters:

```text
EVIDENCE FILTER
How well established is it?

UTILITY FILTER
Could anything in it improve Genesis?
```

A fictional idea can have:

```text
EVIDENCE = FICTIONAL
UTILITY = VERY HIGH
```

while a real technology can have:

```text
EVIDENCE = DEMONSTRATED
UTILITY = LOW
```

Do not confuse the two axes.

---

# 1352. EVIDENCE CLASSES

Use:

```text
E0  FICTIONAL

E1  UNSUBSTANTIATED CLAIM /
    CONJECTURE

E2  THEORETICALLY PLAUSIBLE

E3  EARLY EXPERIMENTAL

E4  REPEATED EXPERIMENTAL

E5  ENGINEERING DEMONSTRATION

E6  DEPLOYED TECHNOLOGY

E7  MATURE / WELL-ESTABLISHED
```

Evidence class can change with new information.

---

# 1353. GENESIS UTILITY CLASSES

Use independently:

```text
U0  NO IDENTIFIED VALUE

U1  CAUTIONARY / FAILURE LESSON

U2  CONCEPTUAL INSPIRATION

U3  RESEARCH CANDIDATE

U4  PROTOTYPE CANDIDATE

U5  DIRECT ARCHITECTURAL VALUE

U6  IMPLEMENTATION CANDIDATE
```

---

# 1354. REJECTED-IDEA REGISTRY

Create:

```text
RES-DEADEND
```

Do **not** simply throw rejected research away.

Record:

```text
idea_id
idea
source
date investigated
reason investigated

evidence found
tests performed
reason rejected

useful fragments
what would cause reconsideration
related ideas
```

Therefore Genesis can later answer:

> “We investigated that already. Here is why it was rejected.”

---

# 1355. REJECTED DOES NOT MEAN PERMANENTLY IMPOSSIBLE

Possible status:

```text
REJECTED_CURRENTLY

INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE

FAILED_PROTOTYPE

PHYSICALLY_UNSUPPORTED

COMPUTATIONALLY_INEFFICIENT

DUPLICATES_EXISTING_SYSTEM

USELESS_FOR_GENESIS

RESEARCH_AGAIN_IF_NEW_EVIDENCE
```

---

# 1356. FICTION TECHNOLOGY EXTRACTION

Do not copy copyrighted implementations.

Extract:

```text
problem being solved
system architecture
information flow
control philosophy
identity model
memory model
body relationship
network model
failure mode
benefit
danger
real-world analogue
Genesis applicability
```

---

# 1357. FICTION IS A DESIGN LABORATORY

Treat fictional systems as large thought experiments.

Ask:

```text
What problem was the author solving?

What architecture is implied?

Which part already exists in reality?

Which part violates known physics?

Which part could be simulated?

Which part can be transformed into
a completely different practical system?
```

---

# 1358. FICTIONAL FAILURE MODES ARE AS IMPORTANT AS CAPABILITIES

For each fictional system extract:

```text
WHAT WORKED

WHAT FAILED

WHY IT FAILED

WHAT GENESIS SHOULD KEEP

WHAT GENESIS SHOULD AVOID
```

---

# 1359. INITIAL HIGH-VALUE FICTION MINING — NEW FINDINGS

The following are **additional** useful sources beyond the material already present in the Genesis specification.

---

## 1360. EVANGELION — MAGI

The MAGI concept uses multiple personality-derived decision systems capable of reaching different conclusions rather than pretending that one computational perspective is always correct. That directly suggests a Genesis decision mechanism in which independent cognitive evaluators can disagree before high-impact decisions are made. ([Evangelion Wiki][2])

Extract:

```text
multiple reasoning perspectives
         ↓
independent conclusions
         ↓
agreement/disagreement
         ↓
decision policy
```

Genesis application:

```text
COG-PERSPECTIVE-COUNCIL
```

Potential evaluators:

```text
logical
social
affective
long-term consequence
empirical
adversarial
```

Do **not** hard-code three fake human personalities merely to imitate MAGI.

Extract the principle.

### Utility

```text
E0 FICTIONAL
U5 DIRECT ARCHITECTURAL VALUE
```

---

# 1361. PERSON OF INTEREST — THE MACHINE / SAMARITAN

This is especially valuable as a **comparison of two autonomy philosophies**. Research discussing the series contrasts the Machine's reliance on humans for contextual judgment with Samaritan's tendency to both classify situations and determine/manipulate the resulting action. ([Sage Journals][3])

Genesis lesson:

```text
PERCEPTION
    ↓
PREDICTION
    ↓
RISK ASSESSMENT
```

must remain distinguishable from:

```text
AUTHORITY TO ACT
```

Therefore:

```text
PREDICTING X
!=
HAVING AUTHORITY TO DO Y
```

### Utility

```text
E0
U5
```

---

# 1362. HORIZON — GAIA AND SUBORDINATE FUNCTIONS

GAIA's architecture is unusually relevant to Genesis: a coordinating intelligence delegates specialist functions, including machine design, environmental restoration and knowledge functions. The fiction also demonstrates the architectural danger of specialist functions becoming independently goal-driven when separated from the coordinating system. ([Horizon Fandom][4])

Extract:

```text
MASTER ORGANISM
        │
 ┌──────┼──────┐
 │      │      │
specialized functional systems
```

but require:

```text
shared identity
shared consequence model
capability boundaries
goal provenance
coordination contract
```

This reinforces the Genesis:

```text
MASTER + SPECIALIST ORGAN
```

architecture.

### Utility

```text
E0
U6
```

---

# 1363. BATTLESTAR GALACTICA — RESURRECTION

Cylon resurrection explores a problem Genesis already cares about:

```text
backup
copy
continuation
replacement body
identity
```

The fiction explicitly separates physical body destruction from transfer/copy into another body, making it valuable as a thought experiment for Genesis's `restore ≠ clone ≠ child` distinction. ([Galactica Wiki][5])

Engineering extraction:

```text
ACTIVE INSTANCE
      ↓
STATE TRANSFER
      ↓
NEW EMBODIMENT
```

Questions:

```text
Was the old instance still active?

Was state duplicated?

Was continuity transactional?

Can two versions exist simultaneously?
```

### Utility

```text
E0
U5
```

---

# 1364. PANTHEON — UPLOADED INTELLIGENCE

Pantheon explores digital minds as software processes, copies, accelerated processing and identity divergence. Its upload mechanism is fictional, but the software-side consequences are directly useful for Genesis. ([Pantheon AMC][6])

Extract:

```text
PROCESSING SPEED
!=
SUBJECTIVE / WORLD CLOCK

COPY
!=
CONTINUATION

SOFTWARE LOCATION
!=
IDENTITY
```

Add research requirement:

```text
SIM-COGNITIVE-TIME
```

Genesis simulation should support:

```text
0.1x
1x
10x
100x
```

logical processing rates where technically possible while tracking the actual time domain.

### Utility

```text
E0
U5
```

---

# 1365. BLACK MIRROR — DIGITAL COPY SCENARIOS

Black Mirror repeatedly explores digital copies, simulated persons and radically altered simulated time. Philosophical research on the series specifically discusses “cookies,” virtual copies and uploaded individuals as consciousness/identity thought experiments. ([Wiley Online Library][7])

Genesis extraction:

```text
SIMULATED AGENT
+
MEMORY
+
PERSISTENT SELF MODEL
```

may create consequences very different from:

```text
temporary mathematical model
```

Therefore Genesis must track:

```text
agent persistence
identity status
simulation duration
memory continuity
affective capability
```

before treating persistent rich simulations as disposable objects.

No claim is made that such software is conscious.

### Utility

```text
E0
U4
```

---

# 1366. SERIAL EXPERIMENTS LAIN — THE WIRED

The Wired provides several useful architectural warnings: identity becoming distributed across a network, a protocol itself modifying the relation between user and network, and a digital presence becoming confused with the physical individual. ([Lain Wiki][8])

Extract:

```text
NETWORK PRESENCE
!=
PHYSICAL SELF

ONLINE IDENTITY
!=
ORGANISM IDENTITY

PROTOCOL
CAN AFFECT
IDENTITY EXPOSURE
```

This strengthens:

```text
NET-IDENTITY-FIREWALL
```

and:

```text
PROTOCOL != TRUST
```

The show's Schumann-resonance/collective-consciousness mechanism belongs in speculative/fictional research, not the Genesis baseline.

### Utility

```text
network lessons       U5
fictional mechanism   U1/U2
```

---

# 1367. NEUROMANCER — COMPLEMENTARY AIs

`Wintermute` and `Neuromancer` are depicted as complementary AIs with different strengths, constraints and a possible combined form. ([Neuromancer Atlas][9])

Genesis abstraction:

```text
ONE COGNITIVE ORGAN
NEED NOT DO EVERYTHING
```

Example:

```text
strategic/predictive subsystem
          +
identity/memory/representation subsystem
          ↓
coordinated cognition
```

This supports specialist cognitive architectures without fragmenting organism identity.

### Utility

```text
E0
U4
```

# 1368. DUNE — THINKING-MACHINE DEPENDENCY

Dune is more valuable to Genesis as a **failure/civilization lesson** than as a literal architecture: the setting treats overdependence on thinking machines as a fundamental societal failure and subsequently develops specialized human cognition as compensation. ([Dune Wiki][10])

Genesis extraction:

```text
AUTOMATION
SHOULD NOT
DESTROY USER COMPETENCE
```

Therefore personal assistance should sometimes:

```text

explain
assist
```

rather than always:

```text

```

# 1369. THE ORVILLE — KAYLON

Farscape's Leviathans are living spacecraft that grow and change, while Pilot acts as an integrated control/interpreter between the living vessel and its crew. ([Farscape Wiki][12])
